Rabinowitz Floer homology for Legendrian submanifolds in prequantization bundles
Hanwool Bae, Jungsoo Kang, Sungho Kim

Abstract
Let Y Y be a prequantization bundle over an integral symplectic manifold ( Σ , ω ) (\Sigma,\omega) . Let L L be a closed monotone Lagrangian submanifold that admits a Legendrian lift ℒ \mathcal{L} in Y Y . Under the assumption that the minimal Maslov number N L N_{L} of L L is greater than 2, we define the Rabinowitz Floer homology of ℒ \mathcal{L} . We then establish an isomorphism between the ℤ d \mathbb{Z}_{d} -equivariant Rabinowitz Floer homology of ℒ \mathcal{L} and the quantum homology of L L , where d d is the degree of the covering map ℒ → L \mathcal{L}\to L . Under a more restrictive condition on N L N_{L} , we show that this map is a ring isomorphism. Using this isomorphism, we compute the quantum homology ring of Lagrangian spheres in quadrics and two-step flag manifolds. Furthermore, we investigate the implications of the quantum invertibility of ω \omega for the vanishing of the quantum homology of L L and the obstructions to topologically simple fillings of ℒ \mathcal{L} . We also show that if ( Σ , ω ) (\Sigma,\omega) admits a polarization and L L is disjoint from the Lagrangian trace, the quantum homology of L L vanishes.



1
Introduction

Let
(\Sigma,\omega)
be a closed, connected symplectic manifold. We assume that the cohomology class
[\omega]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{R})
admits an integral lift
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{Z})=\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Sigma)
. For notational simplicity, we omit the coefficient ring when it is
\mathbb{Z}
. We consider the prequantization bundle
\pi:Y\to\Sigma
with the Euler class
-[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and a connection
1
-form
\alpha
satisfying
d\alpha=\pi^{*}\omega
. Note that
\alpha
is a contact form and uniquely determined up to strict contactomorphism.
Let
L
be a closed, connected Lagrangian submanifold of
(\Sigma,\omega)
. Let
\mu_{L}\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Sigma,L)
denote the Maslov class of
L
. We assume that
L
is monotone, namely,
\mu_{L}
and
\omega
are positively proportional on
\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)
. In addition, we impose the following conditions.

(C1)

The holonomy representation of
L
with respect to
\alpha
equals
\mathbb{Z}_{d}\subset S^{1}
for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
.

(C2)

The minimal Maslov number
N_{L}
of
L
satisfies
N_{L}>2
.

If, for example,
\omega(\pi_{2}(\Sigma))\neq 0
and the composition of the boundary map and the Hurewicz map
\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)\to\pi_{1}(L)\to\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)
is surjective, then (C1) holds with
d=\frac{2c_{\Sigma}}{\gcd(2c_{\Sigma},N_{L}m_{\Sigma})}
, where
c_{\Sigma}
is the minimal Chern number of
\Sigma
and
m_{\Sigma}
is the nonnegative generator of
\omega(\pi_{2}(\Sigma))
. In particular, if
\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)
is trivial, then
d=1
. Alternatively, (C1) is satisfied if
L
is homologically monotone; see Section
3.1
.
Under condition (C1),
L
admits a Legendrian lift
\mathcal{L}\subset(Y,\operatorname{ker}\alpha)
such that
\pi|_{\mathcal{L}}:\mathcal{L}\to L
is a
d
-fold covering map.

We denote by
L^{\sigma}
the Lagrangian
L
equipped with a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
. We also write
\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}}
for
\mathcal{L}
equipped with the corresponding lift
\tilde{\sigma}
of
\sigma
; see Remark
3.13
for the precise construction. In this paper, we study the (Lagrangian) Rabinowitz Floer homology
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
, whose chain-level generators are contractible generalized Reeb chords on
(Y,\mathcal{L})
and boundary operators are defined by counting Floer strips in the symplectization
(\mathbb{R}\times Y,\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L})
.
It is worth noting that we do not assume the existence of a Liouville filling of
Y
or a Lagrangian filling of
\mathcal{L}
. Instead, condition (C2) ensures the necessary compactness properties. From the perspective of augmentations, this corresponds to the existence of trivial augmentations for both periodic Reeb orbits and chords, see Remark
A.1
.
In the presence of fillings of
Y
and
L
, this condition can be relaxed to
N_{L}\geq 2
, as discussed in Section
1.5
.

1.1
Main results

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
(\Sigma,L^{\sigma})
and
(Y,\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
be as above.

(a)

The Rabinowitz Floer homology
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
and its
\mathbb{Z}_{d}
-equivariant counterpart
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}^{\mathbb{Z}_{d}}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
are well-defined as
\mathbb{Z}
-modules. Here, the
\mathbb{Z}_{d}
-action is induced by the deck transformations of the covering map
\pi|_{\mathcal{L}}:\mathcal{L}\to L
.

(b)

There exists a
\mathbb{Z}
-module isomorphism between
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}^{\mathbb{Z}_{d}}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
and the quantum homology
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
of
L^{\sigma}
.

(c)

Assume that
N_{L}>\max\{\frac{1}{2}(\operatorname{dim}L+1),2\}
. Then,
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
and
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}^{\mathbb{Z}_{d}}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
are well-defined as rings, and the latter is isomorphic to
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
as a ring.

If we work with
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-coefficients, Theorem
1.1
holds without assuming the existence of a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure on
L
. If we use coefficients other than
\mathbb{Z}
, this will be indicated in the notation; otherwise, all homology groups are taken with
\mathbb{Z}
-coefficients.

Condition (C2) can be weakened in some situations.
Theorem
1.1
remains valid under the weaker condition
N_{L}\geq 2
, provided that
Y
and
\mathcal{L}
admit topologically simple Liouville and exact Lagrangian fillings, respectively, and the lifted relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\tilde{\sigma}
extends to the fillings; see Proposition
1.14
and Proposition
5.2
below. On the other hand, even when there are such fillings, if
\tilde{\sigma}
does not extend to the fillings, then
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
may not have any reasonable relation with the Rabinowitz Floer homology of
\mathcal{L}
;
see Remark
6.26
.

As usual,
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}
is invariant under changes of the contact form and Legendrian isotopy. Let
\alpha^{\prime}
be a contact form supporting the same contact structure as
\alpha
, and let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
be Legendrian isotopic to
\mathcal{L}
. To define
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}
for
(\alpha^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
, we employ a pair consisting of a Liouville cobordism and an exact Lagrangian cobordism whose concave end is
(c\alpha,\mathcal{L})
for a small
c>0
and whose convex end is
(\alpha^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
. We then take the Floer homology for
\vee
-shaped Hamiltonians (see Section
3.3
) on the region associated with
(\alpha^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
cf.
[
CO18
, Section 9.5]
. Since the concave end is
(c\alpha,\mathcal{L})
, compactness is guaranteed by condition (C2) as mentioned above.

Remark 1.2
.

Assume that
\omega
vanishes on
\mathrm{H}_{2}(\Sigma,L)
and that the image of the inclusion-induced map
\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)\to\mathrm{H}_{1}(\Sigma)
is torsion-free. Then, by Lemma
3.2
,
L
admits a Legendrian lift
\mathcal{L}
with
d=1
, and Theorem
1.1
yields the isomorphisms

\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})\cong\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})\cong\mathrm{H}_{*}(L;\mathcal{O})\cong\mathrm{H}^{\operatorname{dim}L-*}(L),

where
\mathcal{O}
denotes the local system of orientations.
Note that
L
may be nonorientable, and we adopt the convention that the quantum homology of
L
recovers the singular cohomology of
L
when there is no
J
-holomorphic disk. In this setting, by Proposition
3.4
, the chain-level generators of
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
are necessarily constant chords on
\mathcal{L}
.

The homology
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
carries a
\mathbb{Z}[T,T^{-1}]
-module or algebra structure, depending on the assumption on
N_{L}
, see Remark
3.19
. This structure corresponds to the Seidel representation adapted to the present setting, cf.
[
Ueb19
]
, and is compatible with the
\mathbb{Z}[T,T^{-1}]
-algebra structure on
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
, see Remark
2.6
.

Recently, the Frobenius algebra structure on Rabinowitz Floer homology has been studied extensively in the seminal works of
[
CHO25
,
CO22
]
. It is natural to expect that the isomorphism in Theorem
1.1
in fact holds at the level of Frobenius algebras. This question will be investigated elsewhere. We also would like to mention the related work
[
KPS24
]
, where correspondences involving Fukaya categories are investigated.

1.2
Examples of computations of QH and RFH

When
\mathcal{L}\to L
is bijective, the isomorphism
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})\cong\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
allows one to compute either homology from the other. We present some examples of this computation.

Let
N
be a Riemannian manifold all of whose geodesics are simple, closed and have the same length. Then the unit cotangent bundle
S^{*}N
of
N
is a prequantization bundle over the space
\Sigma_{N}
of oriented closed geodesics, denoted by
\pi_{S^{*}N}:S^{*}N\to\Sigma_{N}
, and a unit cotangent fiber projects to a Lagrangian sphere
L_{N}:=\pi_{S^{*}N}(S^{*}_{q}N)
. We compute the quantum homology ring of
L_{N}
when
N
is
S^{k}
or
\mathbb{CP}^{k}
using Theorem
1.1
.

In the case of
N=S^{k}
, the base symplectic manifold
\Sigma_{N}
is the smooth quadric

Q^{k-1}:=\{[z_{0}:\dots:z_{k}]\in\mathbb{CP}^{k}\mid z_{0}^{2}+\dots+z_{k}^{2}=0\}

equipped with the restriction of the Fubini-Study form
\omega_{\mathrm{FS}}
, and the Lagrangian sphere
L_{N}
is

L_{S^{k}}=\left\{[i:x_{1}:\dots:x_{k}]\in\mathbb{CP}^{k}\mid\sum_{j=1}^{k}x_{j}^{2}=1,x_{j}\in\mathbb{R}\right\}\subset Q^{k-1}.

(1.1)

We only consider the case
k\geq 3
since
L_{S^{2}}
is a great circle of
Q^{1}=\mathbb{CP}^{1}
whose quantum homology is well-known.
Since
\mathrm{H}^{1}(L_{S^{k}};\mathbb{Z}_{2})=0
, there is a unique (absolute)
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure
\sigma_{0}
. The symplectic form
\omega_{\mathrm{FS}}|_{Q^{k-1}}
defines a cohomology class of
\mathrm{H}^{2}(Q^{k-1};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
. Let
\sigma_{1}
be a unique relative
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure on
L_{S^{k}}
with respect to this cohomology class.

We use the degree shift

\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L_{S^{k}}^{\sigma})[k-1]:=\mathrm{QH}_{*+k-1}(L_{S^{k}}^{\sigma})

so that the product structure on
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L_{S^{k}}^{\sigma})[k-1]
has degree zero.

Proposition 1.3
.

(Cf.
[
KS21a
, Remark 36]
)
There exists a ring isomorphism

\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L_{S^{k}}^{\sigma})[k-1]\cong\mathbb{Z}[x,x^{-1}],

for any
\sigma\in\{\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1}\}
, where
x
has degree
k-1
.

Let
N=\mathbb{CP}^{k}
for
k\geq 2
. The base symplectic manifold
\Sigma_{N}
is the two-step flag manifold

F=F_{1,2}(\mathbb{C}^{k+1}):=\{([z],[w])\in\mathbb{CP}^{k}\times\mathbb{CP}^{k}\mid\langle z,w\rangle=0\}

equipped with
\omega_{F}:=(\omega_{\mathrm{FS}}\oplus-\omega_{\mathrm{FS}})|_{F}
. Here
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the Hermitian inner product. By the Lefschetz hyperplane theorem, we know that
\mathrm{H}^{2}(F;\mathbb{Z}_{2})\cong\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{CP}^{k}\times\mathbb{CP}^{k};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
, which has 4 elements. A unit cotangent fiber of
\mathbb{CP}^{k}
projects to the Lagrangian sphere

L_{\mathbb{CP}^{k}}=\{([z],[w])\in F\mid z_{0}=w_{0}=1,\;z_{i}=-w_{i}\;1\leq i\leq k\},

(1.2)

where
[z]=[z_{0}:\cdots:z_{k}]
and
[w]=[w_{0}:\cdots:w_{k}]
.

Proposition 1.4
.

Let
b\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(F;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
with
b\in\{0,[\omega_{F}]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\}
if
k
is odd, and
b\notin\{0,[\omega_{F}]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\}
if
k
is even.
For the unique relative
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure
\sigma
associated to
b
, there exists a ring isomorphism

\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L_{\mathbb{CP}^{k}}^{\sigma})[2k-1]\cong\mathbb{Z}[x,y,y^{-1}]/(x^{2}),

where
x
and
y
have degrees
1
and
2k
.

The choice of
\sigma
is crucial as the following proposition demonstrates.

Proposition 1.5
.

For the Lagrangian 3-sphere
L_{\mathbb{CP}^{2}}\subset F_{1,2}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
equipped with the unique (absolute)
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure
\sigma_{0}
, we have

\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L_{\mathbb{CP}^{2}}^{\sigma_{0}};\mathbb{K})=0,

where
\mathbb{K}
is any field of characteristic different from
2
.

Proposition
1.4
and Proposition
1.5
are proved in Section
6.1.3
.

Next, we consider the Rabinowitz Floer homology of
\mathbb{RP}^{k}
in
\mathbb{RP}^{2k+1}
. As proved in
[
DRS24
, Theorem 1.10]
, this homology has rank 1 in every degree as a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-module. Our next result extends this computation as follows.

Proposition 1.6
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be
\mathbb{RP}^{k}
in the standard contact
\mathbb{RP}^{2k+1}
for
k\geq 2
. Then,
there exist ring isomorphisms

\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})[k]\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}[x,x^{-1}],\qquad\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})[k]\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}[y,y^{-1}]

where
x
has degree 1 and
y
has degree 2. Here
[k]
refers to the degree shift as above.

Proof.

Since
L=\mathbb{RP}^{k}
in
\mathbb{CP}^{k}
lifts to
\mathcal{L}
in
\mathbb{RP}^{2k+1}
and
N_{L}=k+1>\max\{\frac{1}{2}(k+1),2\}
,
by Theorem
1.1
, we have
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})[k]\cong\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})[k]
, where the latter is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{2}[x,x^{-1}]
.

Let
\tilde{\sigma}
be the lift of a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
on
L
.
To compute
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
, we recall from
[
Zap15
, Section 8]
and
[
KS21b
, Section 3.6]
that
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})[k]
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
in even degrees and
0
in odd degrees. The quantum homology
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(\mathbb{CP}^{k},b)[2k]
twisted by the background class
b\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{CP}^{k};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
of
\sigma
(see
[
KS21b
, Proposition 15]
) is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}[u,u^{-1}]
with
\deg u=2
. Now
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})[k]\cong\mathrm{QH}_{*}({L}^{{\sigma}})[k]\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}[y,y^{-1}]
follows from the fact that the closed-open map
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(\mathbb{CP}^{k},b)[2k]\to\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})[k]
is a nontrivial ring homomorphism.
∎

1.3
Vanishing of QH for certain Lagrangians

The isomorphism in Theorem
1.1
is obtained by establishing a chain-level isomorphism for a suitable choice of auxiliary data. In fact, the proof implies the following result.
Let
Y_{d}\to\Sigma
denote the prequantization bundle with Euler class
-d[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then
L
admits a Legendrian lift
\mathcal{L}_{d}\subset Y_{d}
such that
(Y,\mathcal{L})\to(Y_{d},\mathcal{L}_{d})
is a
d
-fold covering. This yields the isomorphisms
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}^{\mathbb{Z}_{d}}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})\cong\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}}_{d})\cong\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
. The following corollary also immediately follows.

Corollary 1.7
.

Let
(\Sigma,L^{\sigma})
and
(Y,\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
be as in Theorem
1.1
. Then there exist transfer and projection homomorphisms

\tau:\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})\to\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}}),\qquad\wp:\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})\to\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})

such that the composition
\wp\circ\tau
coincides with the scalar multiplication by
d
on the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
. In particular, if
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})=0
, then
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is torsion of order
d
.

Theorem 1.8
.

Let
(\Sigma,L^{\sigma})
and
(Y,\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
be as in Theorem
1.1
, where
\sigma
is a
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure. If
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}
is invertible in the quantum cohomology
\mathrm{QH}^{*}(\Sigma)
of
\Sigma
, then,
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is torsion of order
d
. Moreover, for any prime number
p
, we have
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cong\mathrm{QH}_{*+1}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for all degrees. If
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Q}}
induced by
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}
via the inclusion
\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Q}
is invertible in
\mathrm{QH}^{*}(\Sigma;\mathbb{Q})
, then
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Q})=0
.

The invertibility
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}
is independent of the choice of an integral lift of
[\omega]
.
One case where
L
admits a Legendrian lift
\mathcal{L}
is when
\omega(\pi_{2}(\Sigma))\neq 0
and the map
\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)\to\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)
is surjective as mentioned above. In this setting, one can show without relying on Corollary
1.7
that
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is torsion of order
d=\frac{2c_{\Sigma}}{\gcd(2c_{\Sigma},m_{\Sigma}N_{L})}
.
Theorem
1.8
applies, for instance, to
\Sigma=\mathbb{CP}^{n}
or
\Sigma=\mathbb{CP}^{n}\times X
, where
X
is a symplectically aspherical integral symplectic manifold, and building on this, we revisit some insightful results of
[
Bir06
]
regarding Lagrangian spheres. We provide a full account of these in Section
6.2
.

A similar vanishing property holds for Lagrangian submanifolds that are disjoint from the Lagrangian traces in polarized symplectic manifolds. The notion of the Lagrangian trace was introduced in
[
Bir06
]
, where its role as an obstruction to displaceability was investigated.
To state our next result, which is along these lines, we recall the setting from
[
Bir06
]
, see also
[
Don96
,
Gir17
]
. Let
(X,\Omega,\Sigma)
be a polarized symplectic manifold, meaning that
(X,\Omega)
is a closed symplectic manifold with an integral lift
[\Omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(X)
,
(\Sigma,\omega:=\Omega|_{\Sigma})
is a symplectic hypersurface such that the homology class
[\Sigma]
is Poincaré dual to
k[\Omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and there is a Weinstein domain
(W,\lambda,\varphi)
such that the interior of
W
is symplectomorphic to
X\setminus\Sigma
. Here
\lambda
is a Liouville
1
-form and
\varphi
is a Morse function such that the Liouville vector field
Z
is a gradient-like vector field of
\varphi
. The boundary
Y:=\partial W
is a prequantization bundle over
(\Sigma,k[\Omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}|_{\Sigma})
. The unstable manifold of
Z
at a critical point
p
of
\varphi
of Morse index
\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dim}W
is called a Lagrangian cocore disk, denoted by
\Delta(p)
. The boundary
\partial\Delta(p)
of
\Delta(p)
is a Legendrian sphere in
Y
, and its projection to
\Sigma
is a possibly immersed Lagrangian sphere. The Lagrangian trace refers to the union of such Lagrangian spheres in
\Sigma
for all critical points of
\varphi
with index
\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dim}W
. Equivalently, the Lagrangian trace corresponds to the union of the topological boundaries of
\mathrm{int}(\Delta(p))\subset X\setminus\Sigma
within
X
.
The Lagrangian spheres in (
1.1
) and (
1.2
) are examples of Lagrangian traces.

By the remarkable result in
[
GPS23
,
CDRGG24
]
, Lagrangian cocore disks are generators of the wrapped Fukaya category of
W
. The following theorem shows that their trace also exhibits a rigidity property. We refer to Section
6.3
for the proof.

Theorem 1.9
.

Let
(X,\Omega,\Sigma)
be a polarized symplectic manifold. Let
L
be a closed monotone Lagrangian submanifold of
(\Sigma,\omega)
with
N_{L}\geq 3
. Assume that
L
admits a
d
-fold Legendrian lift in
Y
. If
L
is disjoint from the Lagrangian trace, then for any
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
on
L
,
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is torsion of order
d
and
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Q})=0
.

If
(W,\lambda,\varphi)
is subcritical, the Lagrangian trace is empty. It is proved in
[
BKK24
, Corollary 1.4]
that, under some additional assumptions on
W
, subcriticality implies that
k[\Omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}|_{\Sigma}
is invertible in
\mathrm{QH}^{*}(\Sigma)
(with
k=1
). Thus, in this setting, Theorem
1.9
recovers Theorem
1.8
.

Remark 1.10
.

A key technical lemma used in the proofs of Theorems
1.8
and
1.9
might be of independent interest.
Let
(Y,\alpha)
be a
(2n+1)
-dimensional closed contact manifold, which is not necessarily a prequantization space. Let
\mathcal{L}
be a closed Legendrian submanifold of
Y
equipped with a
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\tilde{\sigma}
. Assume that every contractible periodic Reeb orbit
\gamma
of
Y
and every contractible Reeb chord
c
of
(Y,\mathcal{L})
is nondegenerate and satisfies

\mu_{\mathrm{CZ}}(\gamma)\geq 4-n
and
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c)\geq 3-\frac{n}{2}
.
This index condition corresponds precisely to our assumption
N_{L}\geq 3
in the case of a prequantization bundle.
Then
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)
defined via contractible periodic Reeb orbits and
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
defined via contractible Reeb chords are well-defined without any reference to fillings. Now suppose
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)=0
. In the presence of exact (Lagrangian) fillings for
Y
and
\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}}
, or under a sufficiently strong index condition, the closed-open map would imply
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})=0
. This vanishing does not necessarily hold under the current index condition. However, we show that the distinguished class
1_{\mathrm{RFH}}\in\mathrm{RFH}_{n}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
, which is the image of the unit of
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathcal{L})
under the continuation map, vanishes. See Appendix
A
for details.

1.4
Intersections under Legendrian deformations

A typical consequence of nonvanishing of
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
is that every compactly supported Hamiltonian diffeomorphism on
\mathbb{R}\times Y
admits a Lagrangian leafwise intersection point for
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}
, see
[
AF10
,
Mer14
]
. Applying this
to a Hamiltonian diffeomorphism arising as the lift of a contactomorphism on
Y
, we obtain the following Lagrangian intersection property for a class of perturbations more general than Hamiltonian diffeomorphisms, as investigated in
[
EHS95
,
Ono96
]
.
For the sake of completeness, we provide a sketch of the proof and refer to
[
AM13
,
Mer14
]
for further details.

Corollary 1.11
.

Let
(\Sigma,L)
and
(Y,\mathcal{L})
be as in Theorem
1.1
.
Assume that the covering degree of
\mathcal{L}\to L
is odd and
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})\neq 0
. Then, for any contactomorphism
\varphi
on
Y
isotopic to the identity,
\pi\circ\varphi(\mathcal{L})
intersects
L
.

Proof.

We take a path
\{\psi_{t}\}_{0\leq t\leq 1}
of contactomorphisms from the identity to
\psi:=\varphi^{-1}
. The associated contact Hamiltonian
h_{t}:Y\to\mathbb{R}
is defined by
h_{t}\circ\psi_{t}:=\alpha(\frac{d}{dt}\psi_{t})
.
Then, the symplectization
\tilde{\psi}_{t}
of
\psi_{t}
, i.e.,

\tilde{\psi}_{t}:\mathbb{R}\times Y\to\mathbb{R}\times Y,\qquad\tilde{\psi}_{t}(r,x):=(r\rho_{t}(x)^{-1},\psi_{t}(x)),

where
\rho_{t}:Y\to(0,\infty)
is a smooth function characterized by
\psi_{t}^{*}\alpha=\rho_{t}\alpha
, is generated by the Hamiltonian
rh_{t}(x)
for
(r,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times Y
. We consider a variant of Rabinowitz Floer homology associated with
\tilde{\psi}
, suitably cut off near infinity. Its chain-level generators correspond to chords
\tilde{\gamma}=(a,\gamma):[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}\times Y
such that

\tilde{\gamma}(0)\in\{0\}\times\mathcal{L},\qquad\tilde{\gamma}(1)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L},\qquad[\tilde{\gamma}]=1\;\textrm{in}\;\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}\times Y,\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}),

and
\tilde{\gamma}|_{[0,1/2]}
and
\tilde{\gamma}|_{[1/2,1]}
agree with
\{(0,\phi_{R}^{\tau t}(\gamma(0)))\}_{0\leq t\leq 1}
for some
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and
\{\tilde{\psi}_{t}(\tilde{\gamma}(1/2))\}_{0\leq t\leq 1}
, respectively, up to reparametrization. Here
\phi_{R}^{t}
denotes the Reeb flow on
Y
. This homology, denoted by
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L},\tilde{\psi};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
, is defined under a transversality condition and is, as usual in Floer theory, isomorphic to
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
.

Note that if
\tilde{\gamma}
is a chain-level generator of
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L},\tilde{\psi};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
, then
\pi\circ\gamma(0)\in(\pi\circ\varphi(\mathcal{L}))\cap L
.
Assume for contradiction that
\pi\circ\varphi(\mathcal{L})
does not intersect
L
. Then,
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L},\tilde{\psi};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
vanishes since its chain group is trivial. This contradicts the fact that
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\neq 0
, which is a consequence of our assumption and Corollary
1.7
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 1.12
.

The above proof recovers the multiplicity results established in
[
EHS95
,
Ono96
]
: if
L
admits a bijective Legendrian lift
\mathcal{L}
and
\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)=0
, then
(\pi\circ\psi)(\mathcal{L})
intersects
L
at least
\operatorname{rank}\mathrm{H}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
times, provided the intersections are transverse. This follows from the fact that
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L},\tilde{\psi};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\cong\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\cong\mathrm{H}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
as mentioned in Remark
1.2
, and the observation that, in this setting, the chain-level generators correspond to distinct intersection points.

Corollary 1.13
.

Let
(\Sigma,L)
and
(Y,\mathcal{L})
be as in Theorem
1.1
.
Assume that the covering degree of
\mathcal{L}\to L
is odd and
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})\neq 0
. Then, for any
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
Legendrian isotopic to
\mathcal{L}
and for any contact form supporting the same contact structure as
\alpha
, there exists a Reeb chord between
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.

The above corollary follows from the proof Corollary
1.11
, together with the invariance property of Rabinowitz Floer homology. We refer to
[
AF09
,
DRS24
]
for related quantitative results.
By working with orientations, the conclusions of Corollaries
1.11
and
1.13
remain true for Lagrangians
L
with the degree
\mathcal{L}\to L
being arbitrary, provided that they admit relatively
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structures
\sigma
and satisfy
\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})\neq 0
.

1.5
Topologically simple exact Lagrangian fillings

An exact (or Liouville) filling
W
of
Y
is said to be topologically simple if the inclusion-induced map
\pi_{1}(Y)\to\pi_{1}(W)
is injective and the first Chern class
c_{1}^{TW}
of
TW\to W
vanishes on
\pi_{2}(W)
. Similarly, an exact Lagrangian filling
\mathcal{L}_{W}\subset W
is said to be topologically simple if the inclusion-induced map
\pi_{1}(Y,\mathcal{L})\to\pi_{1}(W,\mathcal{L}_{W})
is injective and the Maslov class
\mu_{\mathcal{L}_{W}}
vanishes on
\pi_{2}(W,\mathcal{L}_{W})
.
In the presence of such fillings, Theorem
1.1
remains valid even when condition (C2) is weakened to
N_{L}\geq 2
.
We state its simplest form below and refer to Proposition
5.2
for the general version.

Proposition 1.14
.

Let
(\Sigma,L)
and
(Y,\mathcal{L})
be as in Theorem
1.1
, with condition (C2) weakened to
N_{L}\geq 2
.
Assume also that
Y
and
\mathcal{L}
admit topologically simple exact (Lagrangian) fillings. Then,
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
and
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}^{\mathbb{Z}_{d}}(\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
are well-defined as
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-modules. Moreover,
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}^{\mathbb{Z}_{d}}(\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\cong\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
holds.

The fillings
W
and
\mathcal{L}_{W}
yield an augmentation for the Rabinowitz Floer homology of
\mathcal{L}
, which is trivial due to the topological simplicity condition. Recently,
[
BCSW24
]
proved that the augmentation variety of
\mathcal{L}
corresponds to the zero set of the disk potential of
L
, which settles
[
DRG23
, Conjecture 9.1]
. This illuminating result suggests that our results might be extended to more general augmentations.

We apply Proposition
1.14
to derive an obstruction to topologically simple exact Lagrangian fillings, provided that the Rabinowitz Floer homology of
(W,\partial W=Y)
generated by contractible periodic orbits vanishes. This vanishing property holds, for instance, under the additional assumption that
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
is invertible and the minimal Chern number
c_{\Sigma}
of
\Sigma
is at least
2
. The following results are proved in Section
6.4
.

Theorem 1.15
.

Let
(\Sigma,L)
and
(Y,\mathcal{L})
be as in Theorem
1.1
, but assume
N_{L}=2
instead of condition (C2). Assume also that
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})\neq 0
,
c_{\Sigma}\geq 2
, and
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
is invertible in
\mathrm{QH}^{*}(\Sigma;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
. Let
W
be a topologically simple exact filling of
Y
. If the covering degree of
\mathcal{L}\to L
is odd, then

\mathcal{L}
does not admit any topologically simple exact Lagrangian filling in
W
.

Note that by Theorem
1.8
, if
N_{L}=2
is replaced by
N_{L}>2
in the theorem, the covering degree is necessarily even. We refer to Section
6.4
for an analogous statement concerning the case where the covering degree of
\mathcal{L}\to L
is even.

Corollary 1.16
.

Let
L
be a closed monotone Lagrangian submanifold of
\mathbb{CP}^{2n}
with the Fubini-Study form. Assume that
N_{L}=2
and
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})\neq 0
. Then the Legendrian lift
\mathcal{L}\subset S^{4n+1}
of
L
does not admit any exact Lagrangian filling with vanishing Maslov class inside the ball
B^{4n+2}
.

The case of Clifford tori is well-understood. It was established in
[
DR11
,
TZ18
]
that the lift of the Clifford torus in
\mathbb{CP}^{2}
admits no exact Lagrangian filling in
B^{6}
. Recently,
[
BCSW24
]
generalized this nonexistence result to all
\mathbb{CP}^{n}
for
n\geq 2
.
It seems possible to extend the above theorem and corollary by investigating augmentations induced by Lagrangian fillings that are not necessarily topologically simple. This will be pursued elsewhere.

J Kang was supported by National Research Foundation of Korea grants NRF-2020R1A5A1016126, RS-2023-00211186, and RS-2025-02217108. S Kim was partially supported by National Key R
\&
D Program of China No. 2023YFA1010500 and NSFC No. 12511540054. J Kang is grateful to Georgios Dimitroglou Rizell and Zhengyi Zhou for valuable comments and discussions.

2
Lagrangian quantum homology

Let
(\Sigma,\omega)
be a closed connected symplectic manifold. Let
L
be a closed connected monotone Lagrangian submanifold. Then
(\Sigma,\omega)
is also monotone, and the monotonicity constant
\tau_{\Sigma}>0
satisfies

c_{1}^{T\Sigma}=\tau_{\Sigma}\omega\;\text{ on }\;\pi_{2}(\Sigma),\qquad\mu_{L}=2\tau_{\Sigma}\omega\;\text{ on }\;\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L),

where
c_{1}^{T\Sigma}
and
\mu_{L}
denote the first Chern class of
\Sigma
and the Maslov class of
L
, respectively. In this section, we assume that the minimal Maslov number
N_{L}
of
L
satisfies
N_{L}\geq 2
, where
N_{L}\in\mathbb{N}=\{1,2,\dots\}
is defined by
\mu_{L}(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L))=N_{L}\mathbb{Z}
if
\mu_{L}(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L))\neq 0
, and we set
N_{L}=\infty
if
\mu_{L}(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L))=0
.

This section recalls the construction of the Lagrangian quantum homology ring of
L
equipped with a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
, following
[
BC07
,
Zap15
]
.

Remark 2.1
.

(Relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure)
The pin groups
\mathrm{Pin}^{+}(n)
and
\mathrm{Pin}^{-}(n)
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
are two different central extensions of the orthogonal group
O(n)
by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\{-1,+1\}
,

1\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}\to\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\wp}}{{\to}}\mathrm{O}(n)\to 1.

Let
Q
be a smooth
n
-dimensional submanifold of a smooth manifold
M
.
Let
\mathcal{V}
be a good cover of
M
, and let
\mathcal{U}
be a good cover of
Q
, which is a refinement of
\mathcal{V}
restricted to
Q
.
We fix a Riemannian metric on
Q
. The frame bundle of the tangent bundle
TQ
of
Q
is an
O(n)
-principal bundle, and this defines a cocycle
h\in\check{\mathrm{C}}^{1}(\mathcal{U};\mathrm{O}(n))
in the
\mathrm{\check{\mathrm{C}}}
ech cochain complex.
It admits a lift
g\in\check{\mathrm{C}}^{1}(\mathcal{U};\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}(n))
, i.e.,
\wp(g)=h
, and
\wp(\delta g)=0
holds since
h
is a cocycle, where
\delta
denotes the coboundary operator. This yields
\delta g\in\check{\mathrm{C}}^{2}(\mathcal{U};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
, where
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\cong\operatorname{ker}\wp
. The cohomology class
w^{\pm}(Q):=[\delta g]\in\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(Q;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
satisfies

w^{+}(Q)=w_{2}^{TQ},\qquad w^{-}(Q)=w_{2}^{TQ}+(w_{1}^{TQ})^{2}

where
w_{1}^{TQ}
and
w_{2}^{TQ}
denote the first and second Stiefel–Whitney classes of
TQ
, respectively. The submanifold
Q
is said to be relatively
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
if
w^{\pm}(Q)
is in the image of the map
\mathrm{H}^{2}(M;\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(Q;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
induced by the inclusion
Q\hookrightarrow M
.

A relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure on
Q
is an equivalence class of a pair

(g,\beta)\in\check{\mathrm{C}}^{1}(\mathcal{U};\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}(n))\times\check{\mathrm{Z}}^{2}(\mathcal{V};\mathbb{Z}_{2}),

where
\check{\mathrm{Z}}^{2}(\mathcal{V};\mathbb{Z}_{2})=\operatorname{ker}\delta|_{\check{\mathrm{C}}^{2}(\mathcal{V};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
,
such that
\wp(g)=h
and
\beta|_{Q}=\delta g
. Here, two pairs
(g_{1},\beta_{1})
and
(g_{2},\beta_{2})
are said to be equivalent if there is
(a,b)\in\check{\mathrm{C}}^{0}(\mathcal{U};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{C}}^{1}(\mathcal{V};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
satisfying
(\delta a\cdot g_{1},\delta b\cdot\beta_{1})=(g_{2},\beta_{2})
.
When
(g,\beta)
represents a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
on
Q
, we call
[\beta]\in\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(\mathcal{V};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\cong\mathrm{H}^{2}(M;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
the background class of
\sigma
.

2.1
Lagrangian quantum complex

For a smooth Morse function
f_{L}
on
L
, we denote by
\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}
the set of critical points of
f_{L}
.
Let
Z_{L}
be a negative gradient-like vector field for
f_{L}
on
L
.
Denoting the time-
t
flow of
Z_{L}
by
\varphi_{Z_{L}}^{t}
, we define the unstable and stable manifolds of
p\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}
with respect to
Z_{L}
by

W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p):=\{x\in L\mid\lim_{t\to-\infty}\varphi_{Z_{L}}^{t}(x)=p\},\qquad W^{s}_{Z_{L}}(p):=\{x\in L\mid\lim_{t\to+\infty}\varphi_{Z_{L}}^{t}(x)=p\},

respectively.
The Morse index
\operatorname{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)
of
p
equals
\operatorname{dim}W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p)
. We assume that
(f_{L},Z_{L})
is a Morse-Smale pair, i.e.,
W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p)\pitchfork W^{s}_{Z_{L}}(q)
for all
p,q\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}
.

Let
\mathcal{J}_{\Sigma}
be the space of
\omega
-compatible almost complex structures on
\Sigma
.
To discuss
J_{\Sigma}
-holomorphic disks for
J_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{J}_{\Sigma}
, we write
\mathbb{D}:=\{z\in\mathbb{C}\mid|z|\leq 1\}
.

Definition 2.2
.

For
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
{\bf A}=(A_{1},\dots,A_{N})\in(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L))^{N}
. Let

\mathcal{N}_{N}({\bf A};J_{\Sigma})=\big\{{\bf w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{N})\big\}

be the moduli space of
N
-tuples of
J_{\Sigma}
-holomorphic disks
w_{i}:(\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D})\to(\Sigma,L)
representing
A_{i}\in\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)
for
1\leq i\leq N
.
We denote by
\mathcal{N}_{N}^{*}({\bf A};J_{\Sigma})
the subspace of
\mathcal{N}_{N}({\bf A};J_{\Sigma})
consisting of simple elements, i.e., each
w_{i}
is either somewhere injective or constant, and
w_{i}(\mathbb{D})\not\subset\bigcup\limits_{j\neq i}w_{j}(\mathbb{D})
for every
1\leq i\leq N
for which
w_{i}
is nonconstant.

Let us abbreviate
\mathcal{D}=(f_{L},Z_{L},J_{\Sigma})
. We consider the evaluation map

\mathrm{ev}:\mathcal{N}_{N}({\bf A};J_{\Sigma})\to L^{2N},\quad\mathbf{w}\mapsto\left(w_{1}(-1),w_{1}(1),\dots,w_{N}(-1),w_{N}(1)\right).

For
p,q\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}
, we define the moduli space of chains of pearls from
q
to
p
by

\mathcal{N}_{N}(q,p;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}):=\left(\mathrm{ev}\right)^{-1}\big(W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(q)\times\Delta_{Z_{L}}^{N-1}\times W^{s}_{Z_{L}}(p)\big),

where

\Delta_{Z_{L}}:=\{(x,\varphi_{Z_{L}}^{t}(x))\in L\times L\mid x\in L\setminus\mathrm{Crit}f_{L},\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}\}.

For
N=1
, this means
\mathcal{N}_{N=1}(q,p;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}):=(\mathrm{ev})^{-1}(W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(q)\times W^{s}_{Z_{L}}(p))
. We further define the subspace of simple chains of pearls from
q
to
p
by

\mathcal{N}^{*}_{N}(q,p;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}):=\mathcal{N}_{N}(q,p;{\bf A};\mathcal{D})\,\cap\,\mathcal{N}_{N}^{*}({\bf A};\mathcal{D}).

Remark 2.3
.

To define the quantum homology of
L
, it suffices to consider
\mathbf{A}
with all
A_{i}
nonzero. We include in the above definition the case where some of the classes
A_{i}
vanish, as this will be relevant in Section
3.4.3
.

From now on, we assume
\mathbf{A}\in(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)\setminus\{0\})^{N}
.
Then, there is a free
\mathbb{R}^{N}
-action on
\mathcal{N}_{N}(q,p;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D})
given by reparametrizing each
w_{i}
by elements of
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathbb{D};\pm 1)
, the group of biholomorphisms of
\mathbb{D}
fixing
\pm 1
.
We denote

\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{N}(q,p;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}):=\mathcal{N}_{N}(q,p;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D})/\mathbb{R}^{N},\qquad\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{N}(q,p;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}):=\mathcal{N}^{*}_{N}(q,p;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D})/\mathbb{R}^{N}.

For the case
N=0
, we set

\mathcal{N}_{N=0}(q,p):=W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(q)\cap W^{s}_{Z_{L}}(p),\qquad\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{N=0}(q,p):=\mathcal{N}_{N=0}(q,p)/\mathbb{R},

where the
\mathbb{R}
-action is given by the flow
\varphi_{Z_{L}}^{t}
.

Proposition 2.4
.

(
[
BC07
, Section 3.1.2]
)
There is a residual set
\mathcal{J}_{(\Sigma,L)}^{\mathrm{reg}}\subset\mathcal{J}_{\Sigma}
such that the following hold for every
J_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{J}_{(\Sigma,L)}^{\mathrm{reg}}
,
p,q\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\mathbf{A}\in(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)\setminus\{0\})^{N}
.

(a)

The moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{N}(q,p;{\bf A};\mathcal{D})
is a smooth manifold of dimension

\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(q)-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)-1+\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A}),\quad\text{where }\;\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A}):=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{N}\mu_{L}(A_{i}).

(b)

If
\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(q)-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)+\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A})\leq 2
, then every element in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{N}(q,p;{\bf A};\mathcal{D})
is simple, i.e.,

\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{N}(q,p;{\bf A};\mathcal{D})=\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{N}(q,p;{\bf A};\mathcal{D}).

Moreover, if
\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(q)-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)+\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A})=1
, then this moduli space is compact.

Next, following
[
Zap15
, Section 4 and Section 7]
, we define the Lagrangian quantum complex of
L
using canonical orientations. We assume that
L
is relatively
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
and fix a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
on
L
.

Let
J_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{J}_{(\Sigma,L)}^{\mathrm{reg}}
.
Let
p\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}
and
w\in C^{\infty}((\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D},{-1}),(\Sigma,L,p))
.
For any connection
\nabla
on
T\Sigma
, we have the pullback bundle
(w^{*}T\Sigma,(w|_{\partial\mathbb{D}})^{*}TL)
equipped with
J_{w}:=w^{*}J_{\Sigma}
and
\nabla_{w}:=w^{*}\nabla
.
For
p>2
, we consider the Cauchy–Riemann operator associated with
J_{w}
:

D_{w}=\nabla^{0,1}_{w}:W^{1,p}((\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D}),(w^{*}T\Sigma,w|_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{*}TL))\to L^{p}(\mathbb{D},\Omega^{0,1}_{\mathbb{D}}\otimes w^{*}T\Sigma),

(2.1)

which is a Fredholm operator.
There is a well-defined evaluation map

\mathrm{ev}_{w,{-1}}:W^{1,p}((\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D}),(w^{*}T\Sigma,w|_{\partial\mathbb{D}}^{*}TL))\to T_{p}L,\qquad\xi\mapsto\xi({-1}).

For a subspace
V
of
T_{p}L
, we define the restriction

D_{w}\#V:=D_{w}|_{\left(\mathrm{ev}_{w,{-1}}\right)^{-1}(V)},

which is again a Fredholm operator. The orientation line
\mathfrak{o}{(p,w)}
is defined as the free
\mathbb{Z}
-module of rank
1
generated by the two orientations of
\det(D_{w}\#T_{p}W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p))
modulo the relation that they sum up to zero.
Furthermore, for any
w,w^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}((\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D},{-1}),(\Sigma,L,p))
with
\mu_{L}(w)=\mu_{L}(w^{\prime})
, we have a canonical isomorphism
\mathfrak{o}(p,w)\cong\mathfrak{o}(p,w^{\prime})
determined by the chosen relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
. See Remark
2.5
below. For
(p,k)\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}\times\mathbb{Z}
, we set

\mathfrak{o}(p,k):=\varinjlim\limits_{w\in\mu_{L}^{-1}(kN_{L})}\mathfrak{o}(p,w),\qquad\deg\mathfrak{o}(p,k):=\operatorname{dim}L-\operatorname{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)-kN_{L}.

(2.2)

Remark 2.5
.

(Isomorphism between orientation lines
[
Zap15
]
)

(i)

For
p\in L
and
A\in\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)
, we consider the following connected space:

C^{\infty}_{A}:=\{w\in C^{\infty}((\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D},{-1}),(\Sigma,L,p))\mid w\text{ represents }{A}\}.

Then, we have the families of Fredholm operators

D_{A}:=(D_{w})_{w\in C^{\infty}_{A}},\qquad D_{A}\#V:=(D_{w}\#V)_{w\in C^{\infty}_{A}},

whose determinant line bundles
\det(D_{A})
and
\det(D_{A}\#V)
are orientable since
L
is relatively
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
, see
[
Zap15
, Lemmas 4.1 and 4.2]
. Thus, there is a canonical isomorphism
\mathfrak{o}(p,w)\cong\mathfrak{o}(p,w^{\prime})
if
w
and
w^{\prime}
represent the same homotopy class in
\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)
.

(ii)

Let
w,w^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}((\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D},{-1}),(\Sigma,L,p))
satisfy
\mu_{L}(w)=\mu_{L}(w^{\prime})
. The glued disk
(-w)\#w^{\prime}
, where
-w
denotes
w
with the reversed orientation, satisfies
\mu_{L}((-w)\#w^{\prime})=0
. Therefore, the orientation of
\det(D_{(-w)\#w^{\prime}}\#0)
is determined by the relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
as we recall in the proof of Proposition
3.14
; see
[
Zap15
, Proposition 7.4]
for a detailed account.
Using the gluing isomorphism together with a deformation of the incidence condition, we obtain an isomorphism

\det(D_{w}\#T_{p}W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p))\otimes\det(D_{(-w)\#w^{\prime}}\#0)\cong\det(D_{w^{\prime}}\#T_{p}W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p)).

Since the orientation of
\det(D_{(-w)\#w^{\prime}}\#0)
is determined by
\sigma
, this yields an isomorphism

\psi^{\sigma}_{(w,w^{\prime})}:\det(D_{w}\#T_{p}W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p))\longrightarrow\det(D_{w^{\prime}}\#T_{p}W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p)),

(2.3)

and hence an isomorphism
\mathfrak{o}(p,w)\to\mathfrak{o}(p,w^{\prime})
.

(iii)

The orientation of
\det(D_{(-w)\#w^{\prime}}\#0)
determined by
\sigma
corresponds to an element of
\det(D_{(-w)\#w^{\prime}}\#0)
up to a positive multiple.
Accordingly,
\psi^{\sigma}_{(w,w^{\prime})}
is well-defined only up to a positive multiple. We shall henceforth consider all maps and commutative diagrams between determinant lines to be defined and understood up to a positive multiple.

We consider
\mathbf{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{N})\in\mathcal{N}_{N}(q,p;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D})
with
\operatorname{ind}_{f_{L}}(q)-\operatorname{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)+\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A})=1
.
Let
w\in C^{\infty}((\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D},{-1}),(\Sigma,L,p))
. We glue the disks
{w}_{1},\dots,{w}_{N},w
together with the integral curves of
Z_{L}
connecting
q,w_{1},\dots,w_{N},p
to obtain
v\in C^{\infty}((\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D},{-1}),(\Sigma,L,q))
. We have

\mu_{L}([v])=\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A})+\mu_{L}([w]).

As established in
[
Zap15
, Section 4.2.2]
, we have an isomorphism

\det(D_{w}\#T_{p}W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p))\cong\det(D_{v}\#T_{q}W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(q)),

which is obtained by gluing the linearized operators associated to
w_{1},\dots,w_{N},w
and deforming the incidence conditions. This induces an isomorphism between the corresponding orientation lines
\mathfrak{o}(p,w)\cong\mathfrak{o}(q,v)
. Furthermore, this isomorphism commutes with the canonical isomorphisms in Remark
2.5
.(ii), which yields an isomorphism

C([\mathbf{w}]):\mathfrak{o}(p,k)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{o}(q,l),

(2.4)

where
k=\mu_{L}(w)/N_{L}
and
l=\mu_{L}(v)/N_{L}
.
As indicated by the notation, reparametrizing
\mathbf{w}
does not affect the isomorphism.
For the case
N=0
, an element
[z]\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{N=0}(q,p)
with
\operatorname{ind}_{f_{L}}(q)-\operatorname{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)=1
induces an isomorphism

C([z]):\mathfrak{o}(p,k)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{o}(q,k),

as in Morse theory, using the orientation of
T_{z}\mathcal{N}_{N=0}(q,p)
given by
Z_{L}
.

For later purposes, we introduce the following space: for
(q,l),(p,k)\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}\times\mathbb{Z}
,

\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}((q,l),(p,k);\mathcal{D}):=\begin{cases}\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{N=0}(q,p)&\text{if }k=l,\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\bigcup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\bigcup_{\mathbf{A}}\,\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{N}(q,p;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D})&\text{if }k\neq l,\end{cases}

(2.5)

where the second union ranges over all
\mathbf{A}\in(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)\setminus\{0\})^{N}
with
\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A})=(l-k)N_{L}
. When
\deg\mathfrak{o}(p,k)-\deg\mathfrak{o}(q,l)=1
, each element
[\mathbf{w}]
in this moduli space induces an isomorphism
C([\mathbf{w}]):\mathfrak{o}(p,k)\to\mathfrak{o}(q,l)
. Henceforth, we also write
[\mathbf{w}]
for
[z]
in the case of
k=l
.

Finally, we define the quantum complex associated with
\mathcal{D}=(f_{L},Z_{L},J_{\Sigma})
. To indicate dependence of the chosen relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
, we write
L^{\sigma}
for the Lagrangian submanifold
L
equipped with
\sigma
. The chain module is defined by

\mathrm{QC}_{*}(L^{\sigma};\mathcal{D}):=\bigoplus_{(p,k)}\mathfrak{o}(p,k),

where the direct sum ranges over all
(p,k)\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}\times\mathbb{Z}
. The boundary map is defined by

\partial_{\mathcal{D}}:\mathrm{QC}_{*}(L^{\sigma};\mathcal{D})\to\mathrm{QC}_{*-1}(L^{\sigma};\mathcal{D}),\qquad\partial_{\mathcal{D}}:=\bigoplus_{(p,q,k,l)}\sum_{[\mathbf{w}]}C([\mathbf{w}]).

(2.6)

The direct sum ranges over all
(p,k),(q,l)\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}\times\mathbb{Z}
with
\deg\mathfrak{o}(p,k)-\deg\mathfrak{o}(q,l)=1
. The sum runs over all
[\mathbf{w}]\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}((q,l),(p,k);\mathcal{D})
. The boundary map
\partial_{\mathcal{D}}
squares to zero, see
[
Zap15
, Section 4.2.2 and Section 7.3]
.
The quantum homology of
L^{\sigma}
is defined by

\mathrm{QH}_{i}(L^{\sigma}):=\mathrm{H}_{i}(\mathrm{QC}_{*}(L^{\sigma};\mathcal{D}),\partial_{\mathcal{D}}),

and it is independent of the choice of
\mathcal{D}
.

Remark 2.6
.

(Module structure)
Let us assume that
N_{L}<\infty
.
When
N_{L}
is even, the chain complex
\mathrm{QC}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
admits a module structure over the Laurent polynomial ring
\mathbb{Z}[T,T^{-1}]
, where the formal parameter
T
has degree
N_{L}
.
For any
A,B\in\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)
with
\mu_{L}(B)=-N_{L}
, we have an isomorphism

\det(D_{A}\#T_{p}W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p)){\longrightarrow}\det(D_{A\cdot B}\#T_{p}W^{u}_{Z_{L}}(p))

defined in the same way as the one in (
2.3
), which exists under the condition
N_{L}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
, see
[
Zap15
, Proposition 7.4]
. This commutes with the isomorphism in (
2.3
), and therefore we obtain an isomorphism
T:\mathfrak{o}(p,k)\to\mathfrak{o}(p,k-1)

for every
(p,k)\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}\times\mathbb{Z}
. It commutes with the boundary map
\partial_{\mathcal{D}}
, thus induces a
\mathbb{Z}[T,T^{-1}]
-module structure on
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
.

If
L
is nonorientable and
N_{L}
is odd, there is still a
\mathbb{Z}[T,T^{-1}]
-module structure, but with
\deg T=2N_{L}
in general. We refer to
[
KS21b
, Lemma 14]
for a detailed account.

2.2
Product structure of Lagrangian quantum homology

In this section, we provide a brief overview of the quantum product on
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
; for details, see
[
BC07
, Section 5.2]
and
[
Zap15
, Section 4.2.3]
.
Let
\mathcal{D}^{P}=(f_{L},Z^{1}_{L},Z^{2}_{L},Z^{3}_{L},J_{\Sigma})
be a tuple consisting of a smooth Morse function
f_{L}
on
L
, negative gradient-like vector fields
Z^{1}_{L},Z^{2}_{L},Z^{3}_{L}
for
f_{L}
, each forming a Morse-Smale pair with
f_{L}
, and
J_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{J}_{\Sigma}
.
We denote

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{N}=(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3})\in(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})^{3},\qquad N=N_{1}+N_{2}+N_{3},\\[2.15277pt]
&\mathbf{A}=(A_{1},\dots,A_{N_{1}},A,A_{N_{1}+1},\dots,A_{N})\in(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L))^{N+1},\quad A_{i}\neq 0\;\;\forall i\in\{1,\dots,N\}.\end{split}

(2.7)

For
\mathbf{w}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{N_{1}},w,w_{N_{1}+1},\dots,w_{N})\in\mathcal{N}_{N+1}(\mathbf{A};J_{\Sigma})
,
we define

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}_{P}:\mathcal{N}_{N+1}(\mathbf{A};J_{\Sigma})\longrightarrow L^{2N+3}

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}_{P}(\mathbf{w}):=\big(w_{1}(-1),w_{1}(1),\dots,w_{N_{1}}(-1),w_{N_{1}}(1),w(-1),

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59596ptw(e^{-\frac{\pi i}{3}}),w_{N_{1}+1}(-1),w_{N_{1}+1}(1),\dots,w_{N_{1}+N_{2}}(-1),w_{N_{1}+N_{2}}(1),

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59596ptw(e^{\frac{\pi i}{3}}),w_{N_{1}+N_{2}+1}(-1),w_{N_{1}+N_{2}+1}(1),\dots,w_{N}(-1),w_{N}(1)\big).

Definition 2.7
.

For
p,q,r\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}
,
we define the moduli space

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}^{P}):=(\mathrm{ev}_{P})^{-1}\Big(W^{u}_{Z^{1}_{L}}(r)\times\Delta_{Z^{1}_{L}}^{N_{1}}\times\Delta_{Z^{2}_{L}}^{N_{2}}\times W^{s}_{Z^{2}_{L}}(p)\times\Delta_{Z^{3}_{L}}^{N_{3}}\times W^{s}_{Z^{3}_{L}}(q)\Big),

where

\Delta_{Z^{i}_{L}}:=\big\{(x,\varphi_{Z^{i}_{L}}^{t}(x))\in L\times L\mid x\in(L\setminus\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}),t\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}\big\}\quad\text{for }\,i=1,2,3.

We also consider the subspace
\mathcal{N}^{*}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}^{P})
consisting of
\mathbf{w}
such that
(w_{1},\dots,w_{N_{1}},w)
,

(w,w_{N_{1}+1},\dots,w_{N_{1}+N_{2}})
, and
(w,w_{N_{1}+N_{2}+1},\dots,w_{N})
are simple in the sense of Definition
2.2
.

There is a free
\mathbb{R}^{N}
-action on
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}^{P})
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{N}}^{*}(r,p,q;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}^{P})
given by reparametrizing each nonconstant disk
w_{i}
.
We denote the respective quotient spaces by

\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}^{P}),\qquad\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}^{P}).

In the case
N=0
, where no such action exists, we set
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{N}}={\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{N}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{\mathbf{N}}={\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{\mathbf{N}}
.

Proposition 2.8
.

(
[
BC07
, Section 5.2]
and
[
BC09
, Section 3.3]
)
There is a residual set
\mathcal{J}_{(\Sigma,L)}^{\mathrm{reg},P}\subset\mathcal{J}_{\Sigma}
such that every
J_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{J}_{(\Sigma,L)}^{\mathrm{reg},P}

satisfies the following properties for a generic tuple
(Z^{1}_{L},Z^{2}_{L},Z^{3}_{L})
and for every
p,q,r\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}
,
\mathbf{N}
, and
\mathbf{A}
.

(a)

The moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;{\bf A};\mathcal{D}^{P})
is a smooth manifold of dimension

\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(r)-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(q)+\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A}),\quad{where}\;\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A}):=\mu_{L}(A)+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{N}\mu_{L}(A_{i}).

(b)

If
\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(r)-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(q)+\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A})\leq 1
, then every element in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;{\bf A};\mathcal{D}^{P})
is simple, i.e.,

\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;{\bf A};\mathcal{D}^{P})=\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;{\bf A};\mathcal{D}^{P}).

Moreover, if
\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(r)-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(q)+\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A})=0
, it is compact.

Let
J_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{J}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{(\Sigma,L)}\cap\mathcal{J}^{\mathrm{reg},P}_{(\Sigma,L)}
. For generic
(Z^{1}_{L},Z^{2}_{L},Z^{3}_{L})
, denote
\mathcal{D}_{i}=(f_{L},Z^{i}_{L},J_{\Sigma})
for
i=1,2,3
. An element
[\mathbf{w}]\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}^{P})
with

\operatorname{ind}_{f_{L}}(r)-\operatorname{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)-\operatorname{ind}_{f_{L}}(q)+\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A})=0

induces an isomorphism

C([\mathbf{w}]):\mathfrak{o}(p,k)\otimes\mathfrak{o}(q,k^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{o}(r,k^{\prime\prime}),

via an argument analogous to that used for the map in (
2.4
). Here
k^{\prime\prime}=k+k^{\prime}+\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A})/N_{L}
.
At the chain level, the quantum product is defined by

\star_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2},\mathcal{D}_{3}}:=\bigoplus\sum C([\mathbf{w}]):\mathrm{QC}_{l}(L^{\sigma};\mathcal{D}_{2})\otimes\mathrm{QC}_{m}(L^{\sigma};\mathcal{D}_{3})\to\mathrm{QC}_{l+m-\operatorname{dim}L}(L^{\sigma};\mathcal{D}_{1})

The direct sum runs over all
(p,q,r,k,k^{\prime},k^{\prime\prime})\in(\mathrm{Crit}f_{L})^{3}\times\mathbb{Z}^{3}
with
\deg\mathfrak{o}(p,k)+\deg\mathfrak{o}(q,k^{\prime})-\deg\mathfrak{o}(r,k^{\prime\prime})=\operatorname{dim}L
.
The sum ranges over
[\mathbf{w}]\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{\mathbf{N}}(r,p,q;\mathbf{A};\mathcal{D}^{P})
for all
\mathbf{N}
and
\mathbf{A}
satisfying
\mu_{L}(\mathbf{A})=N_{L}(k^{\prime\prime}-k-k^{\prime})
.
The map
\star_{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2},\mathcal{D}_{3}}
is a chain map and thus induces

\star:\mathrm{QH}_{l}(L^{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{QH}_{m}(L^{\sigma})\longrightarrow\mathrm{QH}_{l+m-\operatorname{dim}L}(L^{\sigma}).

This quantum product structure is associative and unital. Moreover, it is compatible with the
\mathbb{Z}[T,T^{-1}]
-module structure described in Remark
2.6
, and thus endows
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
with the structure of a
\mathbb{Z}[T,T^{-1}]
-algebra. Note that, by our convention,
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})\cong\mathrm{H}_{*}(L;\mathcal{O})\cong\mathrm{H}^{\operatorname{dim}L-*}(L)
as rings in the absence of quantum contributions, where
\mathcal{O}
denotes the local system of orientations.

3
Lagrangian Rabinowitz Floer homology

Let
(\Sigma,\omega)
and
L
be as in the previous section. From now on, we assume that the cohomology class
[\omega]
is in the image of
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{R})
induced by
\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}
. We fix an integral lift
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{Z})
of
[\omega]
and consider a prequantization bundle
(Y,\alpha)
of
(\Sigma,[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}})
. This means that there is a principal circle bundle
\pi:Y\longrightarrow\Sigma

with Euler class
e_{Y}=-[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, using the convention
S^{1}=\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
for the circle.
The 1-form
\alpha
is a connection form satisfying
\pi^{*}\omega=d\alpha
. It follows that
\alpha
is a contact form whose associated Reeb vector field
R
is tangent to the fibers and generates a 1-periodic flow. The condition
\pi^{*}\omega=d\alpha
determines
\alpha
uniquely up to strict contactomorphism.

3.1
Legendrian lift

Since
\omega
vanishes on
L
, the parallel transport with respect to
\alpha
gives rise to a holonomy representation

\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}:\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)\longrightarrow S^{1}=\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}.

From now on, we assume that the image of
\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}
is a finite cyclic subgroup, i.e., there exists
d\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}(\mathrm{H}_{1}(L))=\Big(\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{Z}\Big)/\,\mathbb{Z}.

(3.1)

Since
d\alpha=\pi^{*}\omega
, this yields
\omega(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L))\subset\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{Z}
. Under this assumption,
L
lifts to a Legendrian submanifold
\mathcal{L}
in
(Y,\alpha)
such that the covering map

\pi|_{\mathcal{L}}:\mathcal{L}\longrightarrow L

has deck transformation group
\mathbb{Z}_{d}
generated by the time-
\frac{1}{d}
Reeb flow
\phi_{R}^{1/{d}}
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Assume that
\omega(\pi_{2}(\Sigma))\neq 0
and the composition
\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)\to\pi_{1}(L)\to\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)
is surjective. Then, the equality in (
3.1
) holds with

d=\frac{2c_{\Sigma}}{\mathrm{gcd}(2c_{\Sigma},N_{L}m_{\Sigma})}

where
m_{\Sigma}
is the positive integer satisfying
\omega(\pi_{2}(\Sigma))=m_{\Sigma}\mathbb{Z}
and
c_{\Sigma}:=\tau_{\Sigma}m_{\Sigma}
is the minimal Chern number of
\Sigma
.

Proof.

For any
a\in\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)
, there is
A\in\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)
such that the map in the statement sends
A
to
a
. We compute

\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}(a)=\omega(A)=\frac{\mu_{L}(A)}{2\tau_{\Sigma}}
, where the first equality follows from
d\alpha=\pi^{*}\omega
and Stokes’ theorem.
Therefore,
\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}
is surjective onto
\frac{N_{L}m_{\Sigma}}{2c_{\Sigma}}\mathbb{Z}
modulo
\mathbb{Z}
, and the claim follows.
∎

The inclusion
L\subset\Sigma
induces the long exact sequence

\cdots\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}_{2}(\Sigma,L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{*}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{H}_{1}(\Sigma)\longrightarrow\cdots.

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume that
L
is not necessarily monotone, and suppose that
\omega(\mathrm{H}_{2}(\Sigma,L))\subset\mathbb{Q}
, i.e.
\omega(\mathrm{H}_{2}(\Sigma,L))=\frac{a}{b}\mathbb{Z}
with
\gcd(a,b)=1
. Then, there exists a connection 1-form
\alpha
satisfying
\pi^{*}\omega=d\alpha
such that
\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}
satisfies (
3.1
) with some
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Moreover, if in addition
\iota_{*}(\mathrm{H}_{1}(L))
is torsion-free, then
d=b
.

Proof.

Let
\alpha^{\prime}
be any connection 1-form satisfying
\pi^{*}\omega=d\alpha^{\prime}
.
Then
\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathrm{im\,}\partial)=(\frac{1}{b}\mathbb{Z})/\mathbb{Z}
as in the proof of Lemma
3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be the torsion subgroup of
\mathrm{im\,}\iota_{*}
. We split
\mathrm{im\,}\iota_{*}\cong\mathcal{F}\oplus\mathcal{T}
, where
\mathcal{F}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{r}
.
We also choose a splitting
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\Sigma)\cong\mathcal{F}_{\Sigma}\oplus\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}
, where
\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}
is the torsion subgroup and
\mathcal{F}_{\Sigma}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{s}
for
s\geq r
.
Let
\operatorname{pr}_{\mathcal{F}_{\Sigma}}:\mathrm{H}_{1}(\Sigma)\to\mathcal{F}_{\Sigma}
be the projection associated with this splitting.
Then the restriction
(\operatorname{pr}_{\mathcal{F}_{\Sigma}})|_{\mathcal{F}}
is injective.
By the Smith normal form applied to this injective homomorphism, there exist a basis
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{r}\}
of
\mathcal{F}
, a basis
\{f_{1},\dots,f_{s}\}
of
\mathcal{F}_{\Sigma}
, and positive integers
m_{1},\dots,m_{r}
such that
\operatorname{pr}_{\mathcal{F}_{\Sigma}}(e_{i})=m_{i}f_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,r
.

We choose
\tilde{e}_{i}\in\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)
satisfying
\iota_{*}(\tilde{e}_{i})=e_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,r
, and define a homomorphism

\kappa_{0}:\mathrm{im\,}\iota_{*}\to\mathbb{R},\qquad\kappa_{0}(e_{i})=\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\tilde{e}_{i}),

where we regard
\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\tilde{e}_{i})
as a real number in
[0,1)
. Note that
\kappa_{0}(\mathcal{T})=0
as
\kappa_{0}
is a homomorphism. We then extend
\kappa_{0}
to a homomorphism
\kappa:\mathrm{H}_{1}(\Sigma)\to\mathbb{R}
. Explicitly, we define
\kappa
by

\kappa(\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma})=0,\quad\kappa(f_{i})=\frac{1}{m_{i}}\kappa_{0}(e_{i})\,\text{ for }\,1\leq i\leq r,\quad\kappa(f_{i})=0\,\text{ for }\,r+1\leq i\leq s.

Let
\beta
be a closed 1-form on
\Sigma
representing
\kappa
via
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\Sigma)\cong\mathrm{Hom}(\mathrm{H}_{1}(\Sigma),\mathbb{R})
.

For any
a\in\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)
, we write

a=c_{1}\tilde{e}_{1}+\cdots+c_{r}\tilde{e}_{r}+\mathfrak{t},\qquad c_{1},\dots,c_{r}\in\mathbb{Z},\quad\iota_{*}(\mathfrak{t})\in\mathcal{T}.

The holonomy of
a
with respect to a new connection 1-form
\alpha:=\alpha^{\prime}-\pi^{*}\beta
equals

\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}(a)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{i}\big(\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\tilde{e}_{i})-\kappa(e_{i})\big)+\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{t})-\kappa(\iota_{*}(\mathfrak{t}))=\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{t})\quad\text{mod}\;\mathbb{Z}.

Let
\exp(\mathcal{T})\in\mathbb{N}
be the exponent of the torsion subgroup
\mathcal{T}
, i.e., it is the smallest natural number such that
\exp(\mathcal{T})\eta=0
for any
\eta\in\mathcal{T}
. Then,
\exp(\mathcal{T})\mathfrak{t}\in\mathrm{im\,}\partial
since
\iota_{*}(\exp(\mathcal{T})\mathfrak{t})=\exp(\mathcal{T})\iota_{*}(\mathfrak{t})=0
. Therefore,

\exp(\mathcal{T})\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}(a)=\exp(\mathcal{T})\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{t})=\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\exp(\mathcal{T})\mathfrak{t})\in\Big(\frac{1}{b}\mathbb{Z}\Big)/\mathbb{Z}

This proves that
\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}(a)\in(\frac{1}{\exp(\mathcal{T})b}\mathbb{Z})/\mathbb{Z}
, and hence
\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}(\mathrm{H}_{1}(L))=(\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{Z})/\mathbb{Z}
for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
dividing
\exp(\mathcal{T})b
.

Suppose now that
\mathcal{T}=0
. Then,
\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}(a)=\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{t})
where
\iota_{*}(\mathfrak{t})=0
, or equivalently
\mathfrak{t}\in\mathrm{im}\,\partial
. Therefore,
\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}(\mathrm{H}_{1}(L))=\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathrm{im\,}\partial)=(\frac{1}{b}\mathbb{Z})/\mathbb{Z}
, and this finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 3.3
.

Assume
L
is homologically monotone, i.e.,
\mu_{L}=2\tau_{\Sigma}\omega
holds on
\mathrm{H}_{2}(\Sigma,L)
for some
\tau_{\Sigma}>0
. Then, there exists a connection 1-form
\alpha
satisfying
\pi^{*}\omega=d\alpha
such that
\mathrm{hol}_{L}^{\alpha}
satisfies (
3.1
) with some
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Moreover, if
\iota_{*}(\mathrm{H}_{1}(L))\subset\mathrm{H}_{1}(\Sigma)
is torsion-free, then
d=\frac{2c_{\Sigma}^{h}}{\mathrm{gcd}(2c_{\Sigma}^{h},N_{L}^{h}m_{\Sigma}^{h})}
, where the constants are the homological counterparts of those in Lemma
3.1
, e.g.
m_{\Sigma}^{h}\in\mathbb{N}
is the positive integer satisfying
\omega(\mathrm{H}_{2}(\Sigma))=m_{\Sigma}^{h}\mathbb{Z}
.

3.2
Reeb chords and indices

We denote the fibers of
\pi
and
\pi|_{\mathcal{L}}
over
p\in L\subset\Sigma
by

Y_{p}:=\pi^{-1}(p),\qquad\mathcal{L}_{p}:=Y_{p}\cap\mathcal{L}.

We define the relative winding number of a chord
c:([0,1],\{0,1\})\to(Y_{p},\mathcal{L}_{p})
by

\mathfrak{w}(c):=\deg\big(\tilde{c}:[0,1]/(0\sim 1)\to Y_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{d}\big),

where
\tilde{c}
is a continuous map
\tilde{c}
between circles induced by
c
.

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
m_{L}
be the nonnegative integer satisfying
\omega(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L))=\frac{m_{L}}{d}\mathbb{Z}
, which by monotonicity is given by
m_{L}=\frac{dN_{L}}{2\tau_{\Sigma}}
.
Then
c:([0,1],\{0,1\})\to(Y_{p},\mathcal{L}_{p})
is trivial in
\pi_{1}(Y,\mathcal{L})
if and only if
m_{L}
divides
\mathfrak{w}(c)
.

Proof.

The fibration

(Y_{p},\mathcal{L}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\hookrightarrow}}(Y,\mathcal{L})\to(\Sigma,L)

induces the long exact sequence

\dots\to\pi_{2}(Y,\mathcal{L})\to\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)\xrightarrow{\delta}\pi_{1}(Y_{p},\mathcal{L}_{p})\xrightarrow{i_{*}}\pi_{1}(Y,\mathcal{L})\to\cdots.

(3.2)

Through the isomorphism
\pi_{1}(Y_{p},\mathcal{L}_{p})\cong\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z},\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z})\cong\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}/\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}
, the homotopy class
[c]\in\pi_{1}(Y_{p},\mathcal{L}_{p})
corresponds to
\mathfrak{w}(c)\in\mathbb{Z}
.
On the other hand, for
A\in\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)
,
\delta(A)
corresponds to
-d\cdot e_{Y}(A)=d\cdot\omega(A)
, which yields
\mathrm{im\,}\delta=d\cdot\omega(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L))=m_{L}\mathbb{Z}
. See Remark
3.5
below.
Therefore,
[c]\in\operatorname{ker}i_{*}=\mathrm{im\,}\delta
if and only if
m_{L}
divides
\mathfrak{w}(c)
.
∎

Consider the half-disk

\mathbb{D}_{+}=\{z\in\mathbb{C}\,|\,|z|\leq 1,\mathrm{Im}\ z>0\}
.
The boundary of the closure
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}
decomposes into
\partial\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}=\partial_{1}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\cup\partial_{2}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}
, where

\partial_{1}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}:=\{z\in\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\,|\,|z|=1\},\qquad\partial_{2}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}:=\{z\in\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\,|\,\mathrm{Im}\ z=0\}=[-1,1].

Remark 3.5
.

For a given
u:(\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D},1)\to(\Sigma,L,p)
, the map
u
restricted to
\mathbb{D}\setminus\{1\}\cong\mathbb{D}_{+}
lifts to a map
(\mathbb{D}_{+},\partial_{1}\mathbb{D}_{+})\to(Y,\mathcal{L})
. If we denote the closure of this lifted map by
\tilde{u}:(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+},\partial_{1}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+})\to(Y,\mathcal{L})
, then
\tilde{u}|_{\partial_{2}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}}:[-1,1]\to Y_{p}
.
If we choose our lift to satisfy
\tilde{u}|_{\partial_{2}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}}(-1)\in\mathcal{L}_{p}
, then
\tilde{u}|_{\partial_{2}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}}(1)\in\mathcal{L}_{p}
with
\mathfrak{w}(\tilde{u}|_{\partial_{2}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}})=d\cdot\omega([u])
. This is how the map
\delta
in (
3.2
) is defined.

We now turn to the study of Reeb chords of
\mathcal{L}
. Let
c:([0,1],\{0,1\})\to(Y_{p},\mathcal{L}_{p})
be a generalized Reeb chord, meaning that
\dot{c}(t)=TR(c(t))
for some
T\in\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{Z}
.
Up to reparametrization, such
c
corresponds to an orbit of
R
, an orbit of
-R
, or a constant orbit according as
T>0
,
T<0
, or
T=0
, respectively.
Assume that
c
is trivial in
\pi_{1}(Y,\mathcal{L})
. There is a capping with the opposite orientation, denoted by

\overline{c}:(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+},\partial_{1}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+})\to(Y,\mathcal{L})
, i.e.,

\overline{c}(\tau)=c\left(\frac{1-\tau}{2}\right),\qquad\tau\in[-1,1]=\partial_{2}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}.

(3.3)

Let us denote
n=\operatorname{dim}\mathcal{L}
and
\xi=\operatorname{ker}\alpha
. We choose a symplectic trivialization of
\xi
adapted to
\mathcal{L}
over
\overline{c}
:

\Phi:\overline{c}^{*}\xi\to\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{n},\qquad\Phi(T_{\overline{c}(r)}\mathcal{L})=\mathbb{R}^{n}\quad\forall r\in\partial_{1}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+},

where in the latter we abuse notation by viewing
\Phi
as a map
\xi_{\overline{c}(z)}\to\{z\}\times\mathbb{C}^{n}=\mathbb{C}^{n}
.
Then, using the restriction of
\Phi
to
\partial_{2}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}
, we obtain a path
c^{\Phi}:[0,1]\to\mathrm{Lag}(\mathbb{C}^{n})
of Lagrangian subspaces in
\mathbb{C}^{n}
defined by

c^{\Phi}(t):=\Phi\left(d\phi_{R}^{Tt}(T_{c(0)}\mathcal{L})\right),

where
\Phi
is evaluated over the point
\overline{c}(1-2t)
.
We define
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c,\overline{c})
for a capped chord
(c,\bar{c})
by the Robbin–Salamon index of the path
c^{\Phi}
with respect to the reference Lagrangian subspace
\mathbb{R}^{n}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
, see
[
RS93
, Section 2]
.
This is independent of the choice of a symplectic trivialization of
\overline{c}^{*}\xi
adapted to
\mathcal{L}
.
Note that
c^{\Phi}
is actually a loop of Lagrangian subspaces since
d\phi_{R}^{T}(T_{c(0)}\mathcal{L})=T_{c(1)}\mathcal{L}
, and both
T_{c(0)}\mathcal{L}
and
T_{c(1)}\mathcal{L}
are mapped to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
by
\Phi
. Therefore,
the Robbin–Salamon index of
c^{\Phi}
is simply the Maslov index
\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(c^{\Phi})
of
c^{\Phi}
, i.e.,
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c,\overline{c})=\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(c^{\Phi})
.
We consider the Maslov class

\mu_{\mathcal{L}}:\pi_{2}(Y,\mathcal{L})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z},

where
\mu_{\mathcal{L}}([u])
is the Maslov index of
(u|_{\partial\mathbb{D}})^{*}T\mathcal{L}
defined by a symplectic trivialization of
u^{*}\xi
.
Since
\pi^{*}T\Sigma\cong\xi
and
(\pi|_{\mathcal{L}})^{*}TL\cong T\mathcal{L}
, we have
\pi^{*}\mu_{L}=\mu_{\mathcal{L}}
. By Lemma
3.6
, the index
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c,\overline{c})
is independent of the choice of a capping, and we simply write
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c)=\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c,\overline{c})
.

Lemma 3.6
.

The Maslov class
\mu_{\mathcal{L}}
is zero.

Proof.

For a given smooth map
u:(\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D})\to(Y,\mathcal{L})
,

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathcal{L}}([u])

\displaystyle=\pi^{*}\mu_{L}([u])=\pi^{*}(2\tau_{\Sigma}\omega)([u])=2\tau_{\Sigma}(d\alpha)([u])=2\tau_{\Sigma}\int_{\partial\mathbb{D}}u^{*}\alpha=0.

The last equality follows from the fact that
\mathcal{L}
is a Legendrian submanifold of
(Y,\alpha)
.
∎

The composition
\pi\circ\overline{c}
maps
\partial_{2}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}
to
p
. We interpret this composite map as

\pi\circ\overline{c}:(\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D},-1)\longrightarrow(\Sigma,L,p).

Proposition 3.7
.

For any capped chord
(c,\overline{c})
, we have

\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c)=-\mu_{L}([\pi\circ\overline{c}])=-2\tau_{\Sigma}\omega([\pi\circ\overline{c}])=\frac{2\tau_{\Sigma}\mathfrak{w}(c)}{d}.

Proof.

Let us consider another symplectic trivialization

\Psi:\overline{c}^{*}\xi\to\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{n}

such that

\Psi(d\phi_{R}^{Tt}(T_{c(0)}\mathcal{L}))=\mathbb{R}^{n}\qquad\forall t\in[0,1],

where
\Psi
is evaluated over the point
\overline{c}(1-2t)
.
This gives rise to a loop of Lagrangian subspaces

c^{\Psi}:[0,1]/(0\sim 1)\to\mathrm{Lag}(\mathbb{C}^{n}),\qquad c^{\Psi}(t):=\Psi(T_{\overline{c}(e^{\pi i(1-t)})}\mathcal{L}).

This is indeed a loop since
d\phi_{R}^{T}(T_{c(0)}\mathcal{L})=T_{c(1)}\mathcal{L}
. As observed in
Remark
3.8
below, the Maslov index
\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(c^{\Psi})
of
c^{\Psi}
agrees with
\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(c^{\Phi})=\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c)
.

It remains to verify
\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(c^{\Psi})=-\mu_{L}([\pi\circ\overline{c}])
. Since
\pi^{*}T\Sigma\cong\xi
, the trivialization
\Psi
induces a symplectic trivialization of
(\pi\circ\overline{c})^{*}T\Sigma
, which we denote by
\psi
. We define

((\pi\circ\overline{c})|_{\partial\mathbb{D}})^{\psi}:\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}\to\mathrm{Lag}(\mathbb{C}^{n}),\qquad((\pi\circ\overline{c})|_{\partial\mathbb{D}})^{\psi}(t):=\psi(T_{(\pi\circ\overline{c})(e^{\pi i(1-2t)})}L).

Since
(\pi|_{\mathcal{L}})^{*}TL\cong T\mathcal{L}
, we have
c^{\Psi}=((\pi\circ\overline{c})|_{\partial\mathbb{D}})^{\psi}
. Therefore,

\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(c^{\Psi})=\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(((\pi\circ\overline{c})|_{\partial\mathbb{D}})^{\psi})=-\mu_{L}([\pi\circ\overline{c}]),

where the minus sign appears as we use the opposite orientation of the capping, see (
3.3
).
The last equality in the statement follows from
\frac{1}{d}\mathfrak{w}(c)=-\omega([\pi\circ\overline{c}])
, see Remark
3.5
.
∎

Remark 3.8
.

For two Lagrangian loops
c^{\Phi}
and
c^{\Psi}
in the proof of the preceding proposition,

\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(c^{\Phi})=\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(c^{\Psi})
holds. Indeed, recall that if two Lagrangian loops
\ell_{1}(t)
and
\ell_{2}(t)
satisfy
\ell_{2}(t)=A(t)\ell_{1}(t)
for some loop of symplectic matrices
A(t)
, then
\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(\ell_{2})=2\mu_{\mathrm{CZ}}(A)+\mu_{\mathrm{Mas}}(\ell_{1})
, where
\mu_{\mathrm{CZ}}
denotes the Conley–Zehnder index.
In our situation,
c^{\Phi}
and
c^{\Psi}
are related by the loop of symplectic matrices
\Phi\circ\Psi^{-1}
restricted to
\partial\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}
. Since
(\Phi\circ\Psi^{-1})|_{\partial\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}}
is contractible as this admits an extension to
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}
, its Conley–Zehnder index equals zero.

3.3
Lagrangian Floer chain complex for
\vee
-shaped Hamiltonians

Rabinowitz Floer homology was first introduced in
[
CF09
]
. We adopt the formulation from
[
CFO10
,
CO18
]
using
\vee
-shaped Hamiltonians rather than the Rabinowitz action functional.
In this section, we define the Floer chain complex of the Lagrangian submanifold
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}
in the symplectization
\mathbb{R}\times Y
of
Y
, associated to a
\vee
-shaped Hamiltonian.
From now on, we assume that the minimal Maslov number
N_{L}
of
L
is greater than
2
.
This assumption guarantees the necessary compactness properties of the relevant Floer moduli spaces, even in the absence of a symplectic filling of
Y
or a Lagrangian filling of
\mathcal{L}
.

3.3.1
Hamiltonian chords for
\vee
-shaped Hamiltonians

For a small
\epsilon>0
, we denote by
\mathcal{H}
the set of smooth functions
H:\mathbb{R}\times Y\to\mathbb{R}
such that
H(r,y)=h(e^{r})
for a smooth function
h:\mathbb{R}_{>0}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying

h(1)<0,\qquad\begin{cases}h(\rho)=\text{constant},&\rho\in(0,e^{-\epsilon}),\\[2.15277pt]
h^{\prime\prime}(\rho)<0,&\rho\in(e^{-\epsilon},e^{-\epsilon+\eta}),\\[2.15277pt]
h(\rho)=-a\rho+b^{-},&\rho\in(e^{-\epsilon+\eta},e^{-\eta}),\\[2.15277pt]
h^{\prime\prime}(\rho)>0,&\rho\in(e^{-\eta},e^{\eta}),\\[2.15277pt]
h(\rho)=a\rho+b^{+},&\rho\in(e^{\eta},+\infty),\end{cases}

(3.4)

for some
a\in(0,\infty)\setminus\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{Z}
,
b^{\pm}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\eta\in(0,\frac{\epsilon}{2})
.
For
H\in\mathcal{H}
, consider the action functional

\begin{split}&\mathcal{A}_{H}:C^{\infty}\big(([0,1],\{0,1\}),(\mathbb{R}\times Y,\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L})\big)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\\[4.30554pt]
&\mathcal{A}_{H}(x):=\int_{0}^{1}x^{*}(e^{r}\alpha)-\int_{0}^{1}H(x(t))dt.\end{split}

(3.5)

Critical points of
\mathcal{A}_{H}
are precisely the chords
x
of the Hamiltonian vector field
X_{H}
defined by
\iota_{X_{H}}(d(e^{r}\alpha))=-dH
.
If we write
x=(r_{x},c_{x})
, then
r_{x}
is constant and
c_{x}
satisfies

c_{x}:([0,1],\{0,1\})\to(Y,\mathcal{L}),\qquad\partial_{t}c_{x}=h^{\prime}(e^{r_{x}})R(c_{x}).

Since Reeb chords of
(Y,\mathcal{L})
have actions in
\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{N}
, it holds that
h^{\prime}(e^{r_{x}})\in\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{Z}
.
We are particularly interested in critical points appearing in the region
(-\eta,\eta)\times Y
.
For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define

\mathcal{L}_{k}^{H}:=\big\{x=(r_{x},c_{x})\in\mathrm{Crit}\mathcal{A}_{H}\mid r_{x}\in(-\eta,\eta),\,h^{\prime}(e^{r_{x}})=k/d\big\}.

(3.6)

It is a Morse-Bott critical manifold of
\mathcal{A}_{H}
.
The condition
h^{\prime}(e^{r_{x}})=k/d
is equivalent to the condition that
c_{x}
has relative winding number
k
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{w}(c_{x})=k
. Since
h
is strictly convex on
(e^{-\eta},e^{\eta})
, there is a unique point
r^{H}_{k}\in(-\eta,\eta)
such that

h^{\prime}\big(e^{r^{H}_{k}}\big)=\frac{k}{d}

(3.7)

provided
-a<\frac{k}{d}<a
. The action value of
x\in\mathcal{L}_{k}^{H}
is computed as

\mathcal{A}_{H}(x)=e^{r_{x}}h^{\prime}(e^{r_{x}})-h(e^{r_{x}}).

(3.8)

The space
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{H}
for this
k
is naturally identified with
\mathcal{L}
via

\mathcal{L}_{k}^{H}\cong\mathcal{L},\qquad x=(r^{H}_{k},c_{x})\mapsto c_{x}(0).

(3.9)

Let
x=(r^{H}_{k},c_{x})\in\mathcal{L}_{k}^{H}
for some
k=\mathfrak{w}(c_{x})\in m_{L}\mathbb{Z}
. By the property of
m_{L}
in Proposition
3.4
,
c_{x}
admits a capping
\overline{c}_{x}
in
Y
, and the index
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c_{x},\overline{c}_{x})
defined as in Section
3.2
coincides with
-\mu_{L}([\pi\circ\overline{c}_{x}])
by Proposition
3.7
. Then,

\overline{x}=(r^{H}_{k},\overline{c}_{x}):(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+},\partial_{1}\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+})\to(\mathbb{R}\times Y,\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L})

is a capping of
x
. To define index, we choose a symplectic trivialization

\Phi:\overline{x}^{*}T(\mathbb{R}\times Y)=\overline{x}^{*}((\mathbb{R}\partial_{r}\oplus\mathbb{R}R)\oplus\xi)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times(\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{n}),

where
n=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dim}\Sigma
and
\partial_{r}
denotes the coordinate vector field on the
\mathbb{R}
-factor. This induces

x^{\Phi}:[0,1]\to\mathrm{Lag}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1}),\qquad x^{\Phi}(t)=\Phi(d\varphi_{X_{H}}^{t}(T_{x(0)}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}))),

where
\Phi
is evaluated over the point
\overline{x}(1-2t)
and
\varphi_{X_{H}}^{t}
denotes the time-
t
flow of
X_{H}
.
We denote by
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x,\overline{x})
the Robbin–Salamon index of
x^{\Phi}
with respect to the reference Lagrangian subspace
\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{n}\subset\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{n}
. Since
\mu_{\mathcal{L}}=0
by Lemma
3.6
,
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x,\overline{x})
is independent of the choice of a capping
\overline{x}
of
x
, and we simply write
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x)=\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x,\overline{x})
. In fact, since
H(r,p)=h(e^{r})
with convex
h|_{(e^{-\eta},e^{\eta})}
, it follows that

\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x)=\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c_{x})+\frac{1}{2}=-\mu_{L}([\pi\circ\overline{c}_{x}])+\frac{1}{2}=\frac{2\tau_{\Sigma}k}{d}+\frac{1}{2}

where the second and third equalities are proved in Proposition
3.7
.

Let
(f_{L},Z_{L})
be a Morse-Smale pair as in Section
2.1
. We consider its lift to
\mathcal{L}
given by

f_{\mathcal{L}}:=(\pi|_{\mathcal{L}})^{*}f_{L},\qquad Z_{\mathcal{L}}:=(\pi|_{\mathcal{L}})^{*}Z_{L}.

For each
p\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}
, there exist exactly
d
critical points

p^{1},\;\;p^{2}=\phi_{R}^{\frac{1}{d}}(p^{1}),\;\;\dots,\;\;p^{d}=\phi_{R}^{\frac{d-1}{d}}(p^{1})\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{\mathcal{L}}

lying over
p
, and all of them have the same index, i.e.,
\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(p)=\mathrm{ind}_{f_{\mathcal{L}}}(p^{i})
.
Note that the Morse complex of
(f_{L},Z_{L})
is identical to the
\mathbb{Z}_{d}
-equivariant Morse complex of
(f_{\mathcal{L}},Z_{\mathcal{L}})
.

For each
p\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{L}
, we write

p_{k}^{1},\,\dots\,,p_{k}^{d}\in\mathcal{L}_{k}^{H}

(3.10)

for the Hamiltonian chords corresponding to
p^{1},\dots,p^{d}\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{\mathcal{L}}
, respectively, via the identification
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{H}\cong\mathcal{L}
in (
3.9
). In other words,
p_{k}^{i}
is the Hamiltonian chord with
p_{k}^{i}(0)=(r^{H}_{k},p^{i})
whose
Y
-component has relative winding number
k
for
i=1,\dots,d
.
The Floer chain complex we will define has
p_{k}^{1},\dots,p_{k}^{d}
for
k\in m_{L}\mathbb{Z}
as generators. Throughout the paper, when it is unnecessary to distinguish between
p^{1},\dots,p^{d}
, we simply write
\tilde{p}
. Similarly, we use
\tilde{p}_{k}
to represent
p_{k}^{1},\dots,p_{k}^{d}
.
We define the index of
\tilde{p}_{k}\in\{p_{k}^{1},\dots,p_{k}^{d}\}
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mu(\tilde{p}_{k}):=\operatorname{dim}\mathcal{L}+\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(\tilde{p}_{k})-\frac{1}{2}-\operatorname{ind}_{f_{\mathcal{L}}}(\tilde{p})&=\operatorname{dim}\mathcal{L}+\frac{2\tau_{\Sigma}k}{d}-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{\mathcal{L}}}(\tilde{p})\\
&=\operatorname{dim}L+\frac{kN_{L}}{m_{L}}-\mathrm{ind}_{f_{L}}(p).\end{split}

(3.11)

Our choice of
-\frac{1}{2}
above ensures that the correspondence between the quantum homology of
L
and the Rabinowitz Floer homology of
\mathcal{L}
is degree-preserving.

3.3.2
Moduli space of Floer strips

Let
\mathcal{J}_{Y}
denote the set of cylindrical almost complex structures
J_{Y}
on
\mathbb{R}\times Y
, meaning that the following properties hold:

•

J_{Y}\partial_{r}=R
, where
R
is the Reeb vector field, and
r
is the
\mathbb{R}
-coordinate of
\mathbb{R}\times Y
.

•

J_{Y}
is invariant under translations in the
r
-coordinate.

•

\xi=\operatorname{ker}\alpha
is invariant under
J_{Y}
, and
J_{Y}|_{\xi}
is compatible with
d\alpha|_{\xi}
.

The horizontal subbundle
T^{\mathrm{h}}Y\subset TY
defined by the connection
1
-form
\alpha
agrees with
\xi
and with
\pi^{*}T\Sigma
. Therefore, for each
J_{Y}\in\mathcal{J}_{Y}
, there is
J_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{J}_{\Sigma}
such that
\pi_{*}(J_{Y}|_{\xi})=J_{\Sigma}
. We call
J_{\Sigma}
the horizontal part of
J_{Y}
and, unless otherwise specified, use this notation whenever
J_{Y}
is fixed.

For
(H,J)\in\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{J}_{Y}
, we consider Floer strips, namely smooth maps

\tilde{v}=(b,v):(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1],\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\})\to(\mathbb{R}\times Y,\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L})

that solve the Floer equation

\partial_{s}\tilde{v}+J_{Y}(\tilde{v})(\partial_{t}\tilde{v}-X_{H}(\tilde{v}))=0

(3.12)

on
\mathbb{R}\times(0,1)
, with the asymptotic condition

\mathrm{ev}_{\pm}(\tilde{v})=(\mathrm{ev}_{\pm}(b),\mathrm{ev}_{\pm}(v)):=\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\tilde{v}(s,\cdot)\in\mathrm{Crit}\mathcal{A}_{H}.

The projection
\pi\circ v
of
v
defined on
\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\cong\mathbb{D}\setminus\{\pm 1\}
has finite
\omega
-energy. By the removal of singularity theorem, it extends to a
J_{\Sigma}
-holomorphic map defined on the closed disk
\mathbb{D}
.
By abuse of notation, we continue to write the map defined on
\mathbb{D}
by
\pi\circ v
, i.e.,

\pi\circ v:(\mathbb{D},\partial\mathbb{D})\longrightarrow(\Sigma,L).

(3.13)

Throughout this section, we only consider integers
k,l\in m_{L}\mathbb{Z}
for which
r^{H}_{k},r^{H}_{l}\in(-\eta,\eta)
exist as in (
3.7
).
For a Floer strip
\tilde{v}=(b,v)
, we set

\mathfrak{a}_{\pm}(\tilde{v}):=(\mathrm{ev}_{\pm}(b),\mathfrak{w}(\mathrm{ev}_{\pm}(v))),

(3.14)

where we identify the constant path
\mathrm{ev}_{\pm}(b)
with its value in
\mathbb{R}
.

Definition 3.9
.

Let
\mathbf{A}:=(A_{1},\dots,A_{N})\in(\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)\setminus\{0\})^{N}
for
N\in\mathbb{N}
, and let
k,l\in m_{L}\mathbb{Z}
.
We define the moduli space

\mathcal{M}_{N,l,k}(\mathbf{A};H,J_{Y})=\{\mathbf{v}=(\tilde{v}_{1},\dots,\tilde{v}_{N})\}

of
N
-tuples of Floer strips
\tilde{v}_{i}=(b_{i},v_{i})
with respect to
(H,J_{Y})
such that

•

\pi\circ v_{i}
represents
A_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,N
,

•

(r^{H}_{l},l)=\mathfrak{a}_{-}(\tilde{v}_{1})
,
(r^{H}_{k},k)=\mathfrak{a}_{+}(\tilde{v}_{N})
, and
\mathfrak{a}_{+}(\tilde{v}_{i})=\mathfrak{a}_{-}(\tilde{v}_{i+1})
for
1\leq i\leq N-1
.

We also define the subspace

\mathcal{M}^{*}_{N,l,k}(\mathbf{A};H,J_{Y})\subset\mathcal{M}_{N,l,k}(\mathbf{A};H,J_{Y})

consisting of simple elements
\mathbf{v}
, namely
(\pi\circ v_{1},\dots,\pi\circ v_{N})\in\mathcal{N}^{*}_{N}(\mathbf{A};J_{\Sigma})
.
