denotes the Reeb vector field on
Y
. Since
L
is monotone and
N_{L}\geq 3
,
(\Sigma,\omega)
is also monotone and the minimal Chern number is at least 2 or
\omega(\pi_{2}(\Sigma))=0
. Therefore,
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)
is well-defined.
By
[
BKK24
, Corollary 1.8]
, the invertibility of
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}
implies
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)=0
.
Provided that a unital product structure on
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)
is well-defined and
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
admits a module structure over
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)
, it follows that
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})=0
, and consequently
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is
d
-torsion by Corollary
1.7
.
This holds under the stronger condition
N_{L}>\operatorname{dim}L+3
.

Now consider the case
N_{L}\geq 3
as in the statement.
Let
1_{\mathrm{RFH}}\in\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
be the homology class generated by
o_{q_{0}}
, see (
5.6
). By Proposition
A.3
, we have
1_{\mathrm{RFH}}=0
.
The unit class
1_{\mathrm{QH}}\in\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is represented by the canonical positive generator

o_{q,0}\in\mathfrak{o}(q,0)\subset\mathrm{QC}_{\operatorname{dim}L}(L^{\sigma})

for the unique local minimum
q
of
f_{L}
.
The transfer map
\tau:\mathrm{QC}_{*}(L^{\sigma})\to\mathrm{RFC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
mentioned in Corollary
1.7
is explicitly given by

\tau(o_{q,0})=o_{q^{1}_{0}}+\cdots+o_{q^{d}_{0}}=o_{q_{0}}
. Hence, by Corollary
1.7
,

d\cdot 1_{\mathrm{QH}}=d[o_{q,0}]=\pi\circ\tau([o_{q,0}])=\pi([o_{q_{0}}])=\pi(1_{\mathrm{RFH}})=0.

(6.5)

Since
1_{\mathrm{QH}}
is the unit, this proves that
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is
d
-torsion.
∎

Corollary 6.13
.

Let
L\subset\mathbb{CP}^{n}
be a closed, connected monotone Lagrangian submanifold with
N_{L}\geq 3
and equipped with a
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
.
Then
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is
\frac{2(n+1)}{N_{L}}
-torsion.

Remark 6.14
.

Proposition
6.10
and Theorem
6.12
extend to relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structures by working with the quantum homology of
\Sigma
twisted by the background cohomology class, see
[
KS21b
, Proposition 15.(c)]
.
The quantum homology ring of
\mathbb{CP}^{n}
remains unchanged under such twisting, and thus Corollary
6.13
extends to relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structures. This was proved in
[
KS21b
, Lemma 11]
under the additional assumption
\mathrm{H}^{1}(L;\mathbb{Z})=0
.

To relate the twisted quantum homology of
\Sigma
with the Rabinowitz Floer homology of
Y
along the lines of
[
BKK24
]
, it is necessary to twist the boundary operator of the latter as well.

Proposition 6.15
.

Let
(\Sigma,\omega)
and
L^{\sigma}
be as in Theorem
6.12
. Let
p
be any prime number. Then,
\operatorname{dim}\mathrm{QH}_{i}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\operatorname{dim}\mathrm{QH}_{i+1}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
6.12
, there are positive integers
m_{q,i,1}\leq\dots\leq m_{q,i,\ell(i)}
such that

\mathrm{QH}_{i}(L^{\sigma})\cong\bigoplus_{q|d}\,(\mathbb{Z}_{q^{m_{q,i,1}}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{q^{m_{q,i,\ell(i)}}}),

where the big direct sum runs over all prime numbers
q
dividing
d
.
Moreover, by the universal coefficient theorem, we have for any prime number
p
dividing
d

\begin{split}\mathrm{QH}_{i}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Z}_{p})&\cong\big(\mathrm{QH}_{i}(L^{\sigma})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\big)\oplus\mathrm{Tor}(\mathrm{QH}_{i-1}(L^{\sigma}),\mathbb{Z}_{p})\\[2.15277pt]
&\cong(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{m_{p,i,1}}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p^{m_{p,i,\ell(i)}}})\oplus(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{m_{p,i-1,1}}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p^{m_{p,i-1,\ell(i-1)}}}).\end{split}

(6.6)

Since
\mathrm{CO}(\mathrm{PD}_{\Sigma}([\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}))\in\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is invertible as mentioned in the proof of Proposition
6.10
, we have
\mathrm{QH}_{i}(L^{\sigma})\cong\mathrm{QH}_{i+2}(L^{\sigma})
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. This together with (
6.6
) proves the claim for
p
dividing
d
. If
p
does not divide
d
, we obtain
\mathrm{QH}_{i}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Z}_{p})=0
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

In the rest of this section, we revisit several results from
[
Bir06
]
.
An interesting question raised in
[
Bir06
, Remark in p.283]
(also implicitly in
[
BC01
]
) asks whether the following is true: for a symplectic manifold
(X,\omega_{X})
, if
(\mathbb{CP}^{n}\times X,\omega_{\mathrm{FS}}\oplus\omega_{X})
admits a Lagrangian sphere, then
\operatorname{dim}X=2n+2
. If indeed
\operatorname{dim}X=2n+2
and
\omega_{X}
is sufficiently large, then
\mathbb{CP}^{n}\times X
admits a Lagrangian sphere, see
[
BC01
, Section 4.3]
.

Now we assume that
X
is closed (or geometrically bounded) and symplectically aspherical, i.e., both
c_{1}^{TX}
and
\omega_{X}
vanish on
\pi_{2}(X)
. Then, by
[
BC01
, Theorem A]
, we know that there is no Lagrangian sphere in
\mathbb{CP}^{n}\times X
with
\operatorname{dim}X\leq 2n
.
Moreover, it is proved in
[
Bir06
, Theorem D]
that if
\mathbb{CP}^{n}\times X
has a Lagrangian sphere, then
\operatorname{dim}X+2n+2
is divisible by
4n+4
. See the same reference for related results.
As a consequence of Proposition
6.10
, we obtain the following slight generalization.

Proposition 6.16
.

(Cf.
[
Bir06
, Theorem E]
)
Let
(X,\omega_{X})
be a closed symplectically aspherical symplectic manifold, possibly of dimension
0
. Let
(M,\omega_{M})
be a closed monotone symplectic manifold such that
c_{M}\geq 2
and
[\omega_{M}]\in\mathrm{QH}^{*}(M;\mathbb{R})
is invertible.
If there exists a Lagrangian sphere
L
of
(M\times X,\omega_{M}\oplus\omega_{X})
with
\operatorname{dim}L\geq 2
, then
\operatorname{dim}M+\operatorname{dim}X+2
is divisible by
4c_{M}
.

The proof of Proposition
6.16
relies on the following simple lemma.

Lemma 6.17
.

Let
V
and
W
be two modules over the same ring. Let
f
be an automorphism of
V
, and let
g
be a nilpotent endomorphism of
W
. Then
f\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W}+\mathrm{id}_{V}\otimes g
is an automorphism of
V\otimes W
.

Proof.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
g^{m}=0
. Then, the lemma follows from

(f\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W}+\mathrm{id}_{V}\otimes g)\circ\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{m}f^{-i}\otimes g^{i-1}\Big)=\mathrm{id}_{V}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W}.

∎

Corollary 6.18
.

Let
(X,\omega_{X})
and
(M,\omega_{M})
be as in Proposition
6.16
.
Then,
[\omega_{M}\oplus\omega_{X}]
is invertible in
\mathrm{QH}^{*}(M\times X;\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

Since
(X,\omega_{X})
is symplectically aspherical,
M\times X
is monotone and the map
\star[\omega_{X}]:\mathrm{QH}^{*}(X;\mathbb{R})\to\mathrm{QH}^{*}(X;\mathbb{R})
is nilpotent. By
[
MS12
, Exercise 11.1.19]
, we have

\star[\omega_{M}\oplus\omega_{X}]=\star[\omega_{M}]\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{QH}(X)}+\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{QH}(M)}\otimes\star[\omega_{X}]

through the isomorphism
\mathrm{QH}^{*}(M\times X;\mathbb{R})\cong\mathrm{QH}^{*}(M;\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathrm{QH}^{*}(X;\mathbb{R})
. It is an isomorphism by Lemma
6.17
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.16
.

Note that the minimal Chern number of
M\times X
equals
c_{M}
. Since
\pi_{1}(L)=0
, we have
N_{L}=2c_{M}\geq 3
. Let
\sigma
be a unique
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure on
L
.
On the one hand, the chain module
\mathrm{QC}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{R})
has generators in degrees
\{0,\operatorname{dim}L\}+N_{L}\mathbb{Z}
. On the other hand, Proposition
6.10
and Remark
6.11
imply
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{R})=0
. Hence, for degree reasons,
N_{L}=2c_{M}
divides
\operatorname{dim}L+1=\frac{1}{2}(\operatorname{dim}M+\operatorname{dim}X)+1
. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.19
.

(Cf.
[
Bir06
, Theorem F]
)
Proposition
6.16
and Corollary
6.18
also hold when
(X,\omega_{X})
is monotone with the same monotonicity constant as
(M,\omega_{M})
and
\star([\omega_{X}])
is nilpotent.
In this case, if there is a Lagrangian sphere
L
in
(M\times X,\omega_{M}\oplus\omega_{X})
, then
\operatorname{dim}M+\operatorname{dim}X+2
is divisible by
4c_{M\times X}
.

Corollary 6.20
.

(Cf.
[
Bir06
, Theorem B]
)
Let
(X,\omega_{X})
be a closed symplectically aspherical integral symplectic manifold of dimension
2n+2
. If there is a closed simply connected Lagrangian submanifold
L\subset(\mathbb{CP}^{n}\times X,\omega_{\mathrm{FS}}\oplus\omega_{X})
, then
\mathrm{H}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})\cong\mathrm{H}_{*}(S^{2n+1};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
.
If, in addition,
L
admits a
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure, then the isomorphism holds with
\mathbb{Z}
-coefficients.

Proof.

Since
(X,\omega_{X})
is symplectically aspherical,
(\mathbb{CP}^{n}\times X,\omega_{\mathrm{FS}}\oplus\omega_{X})
is monotone with monotonicity constant
2n+2
.
Since
\pi_{1}(L)
is trivial, we have
N_{L}=2n+2
.
By Proposition
6.10
, Remark
6.11
, and Corollary
6.18
applied to
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-coefficients, we have
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})=0
.
Therefore, for degree reasons,
L
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-homology sphere. The same argument proves the assertion for
\mathbb{Z}
-coefficients. ∎

6.3
Polarized symplectic manifold and Lagrangian trace

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.9
. We begin by recalling the statement of the theorem. Let
(X,\Omega,\Sigma)
be a polarized symplectic manifold. The complement
X\setminus\Sigma
is compactified into a Weinstein domain
W
, whose boundary
Y=\partial W
is a prequantization bundle over
\Sigma
with projection
\pi:Y\to\Sigma
. Let
\varphi
be the associated Morse function on
W
. Let
\Delta(p)\subset W
denote the Lagrangian cocore disk associated to
p\in\mathrm{Crit}\,\varphi
with
\operatorname{ind}_{\varphi}(p)=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dim}W
.
Let
L
be a closed monotone Lagrangian submanifold of
\Sigma
with
N_{L}\geq 3
. We assume that
L
admits a
d
-fold Legendrian lift
\mathcal{L}\subset Y
and
L\cap\pi(\partial\Delta(p))=\emptyset
for all
p\in\mathrm{Crit}\,\varphi
with
\operatorname{ind}_{\varphi}(p)=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dim}W
.
Theorem
1.9
asserts that, for any
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
on
L
,
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is torsion of order
d
.

Proof of Theorem
1.9
.

By assumption, there is an open neighborhood
\mathcal{U}
of
\pi^{-1}(L)
such that
\mathcal{U}\cap\partial\Delta(p)=\emptyset
for all
p\in\mathrm{Crit}\,\varphi
satisfying
\operatorname{ind}_{\varphi}(p)=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dim}W
. Then, there is a subcritical Weinstein subdomain
W_{(+1)}\subset\mathrm{int}(W)
such that
\phi_{Z}^{-\epsilon}(\mathcal{U})\subset\partial W_{(+1)}
for small
\epsilon>0
. Here
\phi_{Z}
denotes the flow of the Liouville vector field
Z
. The boundary
\partial W_{(+1)}
is the result of performing contact
(+1)
-surgery on
\partial W
along
\partial\Delta(p)
. The Reeb flow starting from
\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon}:=\phi_{Z}^{-\epsilon}(\mathcal{L})
is periodic and its image is exactly
\phi_{Z}^{-\epsilon}(\pi^{-1}(L))
. The Weinstein cobordism
V:=W\setminus\mathrm{int}(W_{(+1)})
contains the trivial cobordisms
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}:=\displaystyle\bigcup_{0\leq\tau\leq\epsilon}\phi_{Z}^{-\tau}(\mathcal{L})
and
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}:=\displaystyle\bigcup_{0\leq\tau\leq\epsilon}\phi_{Z}^{-\tau}(\mathcal{U})
.

We denote by
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\partial W)
and
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};\partial W_{(+1)})
the Rabinowitz Floer homology of
\mathcal{L}
in
\partial W
and of
\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon}
in
\partial W_{(+1)}
, respectively. We employ the lift of a
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\sigma
for orientations and suppress this choice from the notation. Both homologies are well-defined for sufficiently stretched almost complex structures as in Section
5.1
since the indices of the Reeb chords are sufficiently large due to the assumption
N_{L}\geq 3
, see Remark
A.1
. Arguing as in
[
CO18
, Proposition 9.19]
, we obtain

\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\partial W)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longleftarrow}}\mathrm{HW}_{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}};V)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};\partial W_{(+1)}).

(6.7)

Here,
\mathrm{HW}_{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}};V)
is the wrapped Floer homology for the Lagrangian cobordism
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\subset V
, whose chain-level generators are Morse critical points in
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
, the Reeb chords of
\mathcal{L}
, and the negative Reeb chords of
\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon}
; see
[
CO18
, Section 8.3]
. Indeed, there is an exact sequence

\cdots\to\mathrm{HW}_{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};V)\to\mathrm{HW}_{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}};V)\to\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};\partial W_{(+1)})\to\mathrm{HW}_{*-1}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};V)\to\cdots

where
\mathrm{HW}_{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};V)
is the homology whose chain-level generators are Morse critical points in
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
, the Reeb chords of
\mathcal{L}
, and the Reeb chords of
\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon}
. By employing a sequence of Hamiltonians which have linear slope in
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
, where the slopes are chosen to avoid the periods of the Reeb chords of
\mathcal{L}
, one can show that
\mathrm{HW}_{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};V)=0
. This proves
\mathrm{HW}_{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}};V)\cong\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};\partial W_{(+1)})
. By a similar argument,
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\partial W)\cong\mathrm{HW}_{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}};V)
also holds, and thus (
6.7
) follows. See
[
CO18
, Proposition 9.19]
for the corresponding proof in the periodic case.

There are maps
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathcal{L})\to\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\partial W)
and
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathcal{L})\to\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};\partial W_{(+1)})
induced by continuation homomorphisms, and we write
1_{\mathrm{RFH}(\mathcal{L};\partial W)}
and
1_{\mathrm{RFH}(\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};\partial W_{(+1)})}
for the image of the unit of
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathcal{L})
under these maps, respectively. Since
W_{(+1)}
is subcritical,
1_{\mathrm{RFH}(\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};\partial W_{(+1)})}
is zero by Proposition
A.3
. Note that in Appendix
A
, we consider contact manifolds that do not necessarily admit exact fillings, provided they satisfy a certain index condition for periodic orbits, see the first condition in (
A.1
). The discussion therein extends straightforwardly to the current setting, where
Y
is equipped with a Weinstein filling
W
. Under the isomorphism
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L};\partial W)\cong\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};\partial W_{(+1)})
,
1_{\mathrm{RFH}(\mathcal{L}_{-\epsilon};\partial W_{(+1)})}
corresponds to
1_{\mathrm{RFH}(\mathcal{L};\partial W)}
, and thus
1_{\mathrm{RFH}(\mathcal{L};\partial W)}
is also zero. Arguing as in (
6.5
), this yields that the unit
1_{\mathrm{QH}}
of
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is
d
-torsion. This completes the proof.
∎

6.4
Topologically simple exact Lagrangian fillings

We again consider a closed, connected symplectic manifold
(\Sigma,\omega)
with an integral lift
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{Z})
. In this section, we assume that

•

the minimal Chern number
c_{\Sigma}
of
\Sigma
satisfies
c_{\Sigma}\geq 2
;

•

the prequantization bundle
(Y,\alpha)
over
(\Sigma,[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}})
admits a topologically simple exact filling
(W,\lambda)
;

•

a closed monotone Lagrangian
L\subset\Sigma
admits a
d
-fold covering Legendrian lift
\mathcal{L}\subset Y
.

We denote by
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
the image of
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}
under the map
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Sigma;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
induced by the nontrivial ring homomorphism
\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

Theorem 6.21
.

Assume that
N_{L}=2
,
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})\neq 0
, and
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
is invertible in
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(\Sigma;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
. If
d
is odd, then
\mathcal{L}
does not admit a topologically simple exact Lagrangian filling in
(W,\lambda)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(W,Y;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
be the Rabinowitz Floer homology generated by generalized periodic Reeb orbits on
Y
contractible in
W
; this was denoted by
\mathrm{SH}_{*}(\partial W)
in
[
BKK24
]
.
By
[
BKK24
, Theorem 1.1 and Remark 1.2]
, the invertibility of
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
with the assumption
c_{\Sigma}\geq 2
implies
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(W,Y;\mathbb{Z}_{2})=0
.

Assume that
\mathcal{L}
admits a topologically simple exact Lagrangian filling
\mathcal{L}_{W}
in
(W,\lambda)
. Then,
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{W},\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
defined in Section
5.1
is a module over
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(W,Y;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
, see
[
Rit13
,
CO18
]
. Therefore,
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{W},\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Z}_{2})=0
and hence, by Corollary
1.7
and Proposition
5.2
,
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
is
d
-torsion.
Since
d
is assumed to be odd, this leads to a contradiction.
∎

Remark 6.22
.

The only place where
c_{\Sigma}\geq 2
is used is in deducing
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(W,Y;\mathbb{Z}_{2})=0
from the invertibility of
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
. We refer to
[
BKK24
, Remark 1.2]
for the necessary and sufficient condition for
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(W,Y;\mathbb{Z}_{2})=0
when
c_{\Sigma}=1
.

Recall from Lemma
3.1
that if the map
\pi_{2}(\Sigma,L)\to\mathrm{H}_{1}(L)
is surjective, then
L
admits a Legendrian lift with the covering degree
d=\frac{2c_{\Sigma}}{\gcd(2c_{\Sigma},m_{\Sigma}N_{L})}
. Thus if
N_{L}=2
and
c_{\Sigma}
is odd, then
d
is odd.
An example of this is an even-dimensional complex projective space.

Corollary 6.23
.

Let
L
be a closed monotone Lagrangian submanifold of
\mathbb{CP}^{2n}
for some
n\geq 1
such that
N_{L}=2
and
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L;\mathbb{Z}_{2})\neq 0
. Then the Legendrian lift
\mathcal{L}
of
L
in
S^{4n+1}=\partial{B}^{4n+2}
does not admit a topologically simple exact Lagrangian filling in
B^{4n+2}
.

By working with orientations, we can extend Theorem
6.21
to the case where
d
is even, and thereby obtain a version of Corollary
6.23
for odd-dimensional complex projective spaces.

Proposition 6.24
.

Let
\sigma
be a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure on
L
, and let
\tilde{\sigma}
be the lift of
\sigma
on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}
. Assume that
N_{L}=2
and that one of the following conditions holds.

(i)

The class
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathrm{QH}^{*}(\Sigma)
is invertible and
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is not
d
-torsion.

(ii)

The class
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Q}}\in\mathrm{QH}^{*}(\Sigma;\mathbb{Q})
, obtained from
[\omega]_{\mathbb{Z}}
via the inclusion
\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Q}
, is invertible, and
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Q})\neq 0
.

Then,
\mathcal{L}
does not admit a topologically simple exact Lagrangian filling in
(W,\lambda)
equipped with a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure that restricts to
\tilde{\sigma}
on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}
, in the sense of (B3) in Section
5
.

Proof.

The proof follows along similar lines to that of Theorem
6.21
. We outline the proof for case (ii).
Assume that
\mathcal{L}
admits a topologically simple exact Lagrangian filling
\mathcal{L}_{W}
in
(W,\lambda)
equipped with a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\hat{\sigma}
satisfying (B3).
Then
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{W}^{\hat{\sigma}},\mathcal{L};\mathbb{Q})=0
, and consequently
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Q})=0
. This contradiction completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 6.25
.

Let
L^{\sigma}
be a closed monotone Lagrangian submanifold of
\mathbb{CP}^{2n+1}
equipped with a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure. Assume that
N_{L}=2
and that either
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma})
is not
(2n+2)
-torsion or
\mathrm{QH}_{*}(L^{\sigma};\mathbb{Q})\neq 0
. Then, the Legendrian lift
\mathcal{L}
of
L
in
S^{4n+3}=\partial{B}^{4n+4}
does not admit a topologically simple exact Lagrangian filling equipped with a relative
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure that restricts to
\tilde{\sigma}
on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}
.

Remark 6.26
.

As proved in
[
Cho04
]
, the Floer cohomology of the Clifford torus
T_{\mathrm{Cl}}\subset\mathbb{CP}^{n}
equipped with the standard
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure
\sigma_{\mathrm{std}}
is isomorphic to its singular cohomology, i.e., in our terminology,

\mathrm{QH}_{*}(T_{\mathrm{Cl}}^{\sigma_{\mathrm{std}}};\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}(T_{\mathrm{Cl}};\mathbb{Q})\otimes\mathbb{Q}[T,T^{-1}].

(6.8)

This isomorphism also holds with
\mathbb{Q}
replaced by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. It is also shown in
[
Cho04
]
that, for even
n
,
T_{\mathrm{Cl}}
has vanishing quantum homology for any nonstandard
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure, whereas, for odd
n
, it admits exactly one nonstandard
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure
\sigma_{\mathrm{nstd}}
for which (
6.8
) holds.

The Legendrian knot
\mathcal{L}\subset S^{3}
that is a double cover of an equator
S^{1}_{\mathrm{eq}}
of
\mathbb{CP}^{1}
admits an exact Lagrangian disk filling
\mathcal{L}_{B^{4}}\subset B^{4}
.
According to Corollary
6.25
and (
6.8
), the
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure
\tilde{\sigma}
on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}
does not extend to
\mathcal{L}_{B^{4}}
, where
\tilde{\sigma}
is the lift of either
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure on
S^{1}_{\mathrm{eq}}
. Indeed,
\tilde{\sigma}
is standard for any choice of
\sigma
due to the two-fold covering
\mathcal{L}\to S^{1}_{\mathrm{eq}}
. In contrast, the unique
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure
\hat{\sigma}
on
\mathcal{L}_{B^{4}}
restricts to a nonstandard one on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}
.

The situation is different for the Legendrian knot
S^{*}_{q}S^{2}\subset S^{*}S^{2}
that is a bijective lift of the equator
S^{1}_{\mathrm{eq}}\subset\mathbb{CP}^{1}
. The unit disk cotangent fiber
D^{*}_{q}S^{2}\subset D^{*}S^{2}
gives an exact Lagrangian filling of
S^{*}_{q}S^{2}
. Let
\hat{\sigma}
be the unique
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure on
D^{*}_{q}S^{2}
. The restriction of
\hat{\sigma}
to
\mathbb{R}\times S_{q}^{*}S^{2}
is the lift
\tilde{\sigma}_{\mathrm{nstd}}
of the nonstandard
\mathrm{Spin}
-structure
{\sigma}_{\mathrm{nstd}}
on
S^{1}_{\mathrm{eq}}
. Theorem
1.1
and Proposition
5.2
yield the following isomorphisms

\mathrm{QH}_{*}((S^{1}_{\mathrm{eq}})^{\sigma_{\mathrm{nstd}}};\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(S_{q}^{*}S^{2};{\tilde{\sigma}_{\mathrm{nstd}}},\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(D_{q}^{*}S^{2},S^{*}_{q}S^{2};{\hat{\sigma}},\mathbb{Q}).

Moreover, by
[
CHO25
, Theorem 1.5]
, we have

\mathrm{RFH}_{j}(D_{q}^{*}S^{2},S^{*}_{q}S^{2};{\hat{\sigma}},\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathrm{H}_{j-1}(\Omega S^{2};\mathbb{Q})\oplus\mathrm{H}^{-j}(\Omega S^{2};\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathbb{Q}\qquad\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}.

Note that the second Stiefel–Whitney class
w_{2}^{TS^{2}}
of
TS^{2}
vanishes.
This computation agrees with the known result for
\mathrm{QH}_{*}((S^{1}_{\mathrm{eq}})^{\sigma_{\mathrm{nstd}}};\mathbb{Q})
mentioned after (
6.8
).

Appendix A
Appendix: Proof of
1_{\mathrm{RFH}}=0

In this section, let
(Y,\alpha)
be a
(2n+1)
-dimensional closed contact manifold, which is not necessarily a prequantization space. Let
\mathcal{L}
be a closed
n
-dimensional Legendrian submanifold of
Y
. Let
\tilde{\sigma}
be a
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}
. For simplicity, we assume that all contractible periodic orbits and all contractible chords of the Reeb vector field
R
on
(Y,\mathcal{L})
are nondegenerate; however, our arguments below can be adapted to the Morse-Bott setting. Here contractibility refers to being trivial in
\pi_{1}(Y)
and
\pi_{1}(Y,\mathcal{L})
, respectively. We assume that every contractible periodic Reeb orbit
\gamma
and every contractible Reeb chord
c
satisfy

\mu_{\mathrm{CZ}}(\gamma)\geq 4-n,\qquad\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c)\geq 3-\frac{n}{2}.

(A.1)

Remark A.1
.

These index conditions correspond to
c_{\Sigma}\geq 2
and
N_{L}\geq 3
when
Y
is a prequantization bundle over
\Sigma
with a connection 1-form
\alpha
and
\mathcal{L}
is a lift of a Lagrangian
L
. In this case, Reeb chords appear in
\mathcal{L}
-families. We consider one such family containing a contractible Reeb chord
c
. If we perturb
\alpha
using a small Morse function
f_{\mathcal{L}}
so that this
\mathcal{L}
-family of Reeb chords bifurcates into
\mathrm{Crit}f_{\mathcal{L}}
many nondegenerate Reeb chords with indices
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}
in the range
[\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c)-\frac{n}{2},\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c)+\frac{n}{2}]
. Since
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c)\geq N_{L}
, the latter condition (
A.1
) agrees with our standing assumption
N_{L}\geq 3
.

Our convention on
\mu_{\mathrm{CZ}}(\gamma)
and
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c)
here is that the moduli space of unparametrized finite energy planes resp. half-planes in
(\mathbb{R}\times Y,\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L})
asymptotic to
\gamma
resp.
c
has virtual dimension
\mu_{\mathrm{CZ}}(\gamma)+n-2
resp.
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c)+\frac{n-2}{2}
.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{k}(x_{+},x_{-},c_{1},\dots,c_{k},\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{l};H,J)
be the moduli space of Floer strips
\tilde{v}
satisfying
\mathrm{ev}_{\pm}(\tilde{v})=x_{\pm}
, with
k
negative boundary punctures asymptotic to
c_{1},\dots,c_{k}
and
l
negative interior punctures asymptotic to
\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{l}
.
Then, the virtual dimension of this moduli space equals

\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x_{+})-\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x_{-})-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Big(\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c_{j})+\frac{n-2}{2}\Big)-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\Big(\mu_{\mathrm{CZ}}(\gamma_{j})+{n-2}\Big).

Since there is an
\mathbb{R}
-action translating the domain, if
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x_{+})-\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x_{-})\leq 2
, then this moduli space is empty for a generic choice of
J
by the assumption (
A.1
), cf.
[
Ueb19
, Corollary 3.7]
.

Let
J
be an
S^{1}
-family of almost complex structures on
\mathbb{R}\times Y
which is cylindrical outside a neighborhood of
\{0\}\times Y
. We choose a generic
J
such that the moduli spaces in this section are cut out transversely. Let
H_{a}\in\mathcal{H}
, where the subscript
a
indicates the slope in (
3.4
). Nonconstant chords of
X_{H_{a}}
with endpoints on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}
are nondegenerate, but we still have an
\mathcal{L}
-family of constant chords. Thus we choose an auxiliary Morse function
f_{\mathcal{L}}
on
\mathcal{L}
.

For real numbers
b_{1}<b_{2}
, we define
\mathrm{FH}_{*}^{(b_{1},b_{2})}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{a})
in the same manner as in Sections
3.3.4
and
3.4.2
. Its chain module is the direct sum of the orientation lines of the critical points of
f_{\mathcal{L}}
and the nonconstant chords of
X_{H_{a}}
appearing in
(-\eta,\eta)\times Y
whose
\mathcal{A}_{H_{a}}
-values lie in
(b_{1},b_{2})
. We define the grading
\mu
by
\mu(x)=\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x)+\frac{n-1}{2}
for nonconstant chords
x=(r_{x},c_{x})
and
\mu(c)=n-\operatorname{ind}_{f_{\mathcal{L}}}(c)
for
c\in\mathrm{Crit}f_{\mathcal{L}}
. This convention matches (
3.11
). Recall from Section
3.3.1
that
\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(x)=\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c_{x})+\frac{1}{2}
as
H_{a}
is convex in the
\mathbb{R}
-direction along
x
. Note that
X_{H_{a}}
has chords other than those contained in
(-\eta,\eta)\times Y
. Nevertheless, by
[
CO18
, Lemmas 2.2 and 2.3]
, this chain module generated by chords in
(-\eta,\eta)\times Y
carries a well-defined boundary operator. The index conditions in (
A.1
) exclude any potential bubbling-off of finite energy (half-)planes as explained in Remark
A.1
.
As before
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
is defined as
\varinjlim\limits_{b_{2}\uparrow+\infty}\varprojlim\limits_{b_{1}\downarrow-\infty}\varinjlim\limits_{H_{a}\in\mathcal{H}}\mathrm{FH}_{*}^{(b_{1},b_{2})}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{a})
.

We briefly recall the construction of the Rabinowitz Floer homology
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)
based on periodic orbits, and refer to
[
BKK24
]
for details, where
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)
is denoted by
\mathrm{SH}_{*}(Y)
.
We only consider 1-periodic orbits of
X_{H_{a}}
appearing near
\{0\}\times Y
. These correspond to constant orbits on
Y
and periodic orbits of
R
or
-R
with period less than
a
. We choose a Morse function
f_{Y}
on the space of constant orbits, which is diffeomorphic to
Y
. Each nonconstant orbits appear in
S^{1}
-families. We then perturb
H_{a}
in a time-dependent manner near each such family so that it gives rise to exactly two 1-periodic orbits. For brevity, the perturbed Hamiltonian is again denoted by
H_{a}
.
The Floer chain module
\mathrm{FC}_{*}^{(b_{1},b_{2})}(H_{a})
is the direct sum of the orientation lines associated to nonconstant orbits and critical points of
f_{Y}
whose action values lie in
(b_{1},b_{2})
. Its homology
\mathrm{FH}_{*}^{(b_{1},b_{2})}(H_{a})
is well-defined due to
[
CO18
, Lemmas 2.2 and 2.3]
and the first index condition in (
A.1
). We define the grading
\mu_{Y}
by
\mu_{Y}(q)=\mu_{\mathrm{CZ}}(q)
if
q
is nonconstant and
\mu_{Y}(q)=n+1-\operatorname{ind}_{f_{Y}}(q)
if
q
is constant. Then
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)
is defined as
\varinjlim\limits_{b_{2}\uparrow+\infty}\varprojlim\limits_{b_{1}\downarrow-\infty}\varinjlim\limits_{H_{a}\in\mathcal{H}}\mathrm{FH}_{*}^{(b_{1},b_{2})}(H_{a})
.

Now we describe the moduli space used to define
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)
-module structure on
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
.
Note that this module structure is not well-defined in full generality due to the lack of a filling of
\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}}
. We consider the surface

S^{M}:=\big((\mathbb{R}\times[-1,-\tfrac{1}{2}])\sqcup(\mathbb{R}\times[-\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{1}{2}])\sqcup(\mathbb{R}\times[\tfrac{1}{2},1])\big)/\sim^{M}.

The equivalence relation
\sim^{M}
is given by

•

(s,-\frac{1}{2}^{+})\sim(s,-\frac{1}{2}^{-})
and
(s,\frac{1}{2}^{+})\sim(s,\frac{1}{2}^{-})
for
s\leq 0
,

•

(s,-\frac{1}{2}^{+})\sim(s,\frac{1}{2}^{-})
and
(s,\frac{1}{2}^{+})\sim(s,-\frac{1}{2}^{-})
for
s\geq 0
,

where
-\frac{1}{2}^{-}\in[-1,-\frac{1}{2}]
,
-\frac{1}{2}^{+}\in[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]
,
\frac{1}{2}^{-}\in[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]
, and
\frac{1}{2}^{+}\in[\frac{1}{2},1]
.
As in the case of
S^{P}
in Section
3.5
,
S^{M}
is endowed with a natural complex structure, and the global coordinate
z=s+it
is holomorphic everywhere except at the branching point
(0,-\frac{1}{2}^{\pm})=(0,\frac{1}{2}^{\pm})
.

Let
H_{a_{1}},H_{a_{2}},H_{a_{1}+a_{2}}\in\mathcal{H}
satisfy

H_{a_{1}}+H_{a_{2}}\leq H_{a_{1}+a_{2}}
.
Let
q
be a 1-periodic orbit of
X_{H_{a_{1}}}
, which is either a critical point of
f_{Y}
or a nonconstant orbit. Let
c_{+}
and
c_{-}
be chords of
X_{H_{a_{2}}}
and
X_{H_{a_{1}+a_{2}}}
, respectively. If any of
c_{\pm}
is constant, then we assume that it is a critical point of
f_{\mathcal{L}}
.
Let
(H_{z},J_{z})_{z\in S^{M}}
be a domain-dependent Floer datum which agrees with
(H_{a_{1}},J)
on the positive cylindrical end, and with
(H_{a_{2}},J)
and
(H_{a_{1}+a_{2}},J)
on the positive and negative strip-like ends, respectively. We require the condition
\partial_{s}H_{z}\leq 0
to ensure that the maximum principle holds.
We choose a generic
J_{z}
so that the moduli spaces in (
A.2
) are cut out transversely.
We consider the moduli space

\mathcal{M}(q,c_{+},c_{-};H_{a_{1}},H_{a_{2}},H_{a_{1}+a_{2}})

(A.2)

consisting of smooth maps
\tilde{v}:(S^{M},\partial S^{M})\to(\mathbb{R}\times Y,\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L})
satisfying

\partial_{s}\tilde{v}+J_{z}(\tilde{v})(\partial_{t}\tilde{v}-X_{H_{z}}(\tilde{v}))=0
on the interior
and the following asymptotic conditions:

•

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\tilde{v}(s,t-\tfrac{1}{2})
is
q
or in the stable manifold of
-\nabla f_{Y}
at
q
if
q
is constant,

•

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\tilde{v}(s,t-1+\lfloor 2t\rfloor)
is
c_{+}
or in the stable manifold of
-\nabla f_{\mathcal{L}}
at
c_{+}
if
c_{+}
is constant,

•

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to-\infty}\tilde{v}(s,2t-1)
is
c_{-}
or in the unstable manifold of
-\nabla f_{\mathcal{L}}
at
c_{-}
if
c_{-}
is constant.

Here
\lfloor 2t\rfloor
denotes the greatest integer less than or equal to
2t
.
This moduli space has dimension
\mu_{Y}(q)+\mu(c_{+})-\mu(c_{-})-(n+1)
.
We are particularly interested in the case where
\mu_{Y}(q)=n+2
,
\mu(c_{+})=\mu(c_{-})=n
. Then, this moduli space is 1-dimensional, and its possible Floer-type breaking configurations are as in Figure
4
.

Figure 4
:
Possible configurations of broken curves

The last type of breaking contributes trivially when
c_{+}
is a cycle.
Even though
\mathcal{L}
might not admit an exact Lagrangian filling and the condition in (
A.1
) is not sufficiently strong, we can establish compactness of the above moduli spaces under suitable restrictions on the indices.

Proposition A.2
.

We assume that

\big(\mu_{Y}(q),\mu(c_{+}),\mu(c_{-})\big)\in\big\{(n+2,n,n),(n+1,n,n),(n+2,n,n+1)\big\}.

Then, there exist real numbers
r_{-}<r_{+}
such that the image of every element of the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(q,c_{+},c_{-};H_{a_{1}},H_{a_{2}},H_{a_{1}+a_{2}})
is contained in
(r_{-},r_{+})\times Y
.

Proof.

We argue as in the proof of Proposition
3.24
. Let
\{\tilde{v}_{\nu}\}_{\nu\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence in the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(q,c_{+},c_{-};H_{a_{1}},H_{a_{2}},H_{a_{1}+a_{2}})
. In view of (
A.1
) and Remark
A.1
, no
J
-holomorphic (half-)plane bubbles off in a limit of
\{\tilde{v}_{\nu}\}_{\nu\in\mathbb{N}}
. The only noncompact scenario is illustrated in Figure
5
. While the SFT-limit of
\{\tilde{v}_{\nu}\}_{\nu\in\mathbb{N}}
could consist of more than two levels, our argument remains valid as we derive a contradiction by analyzing the topmost component.

Figure 5
:
Possible configurations of SFT-broken curves

In the first case of Figure
5
, the lower-right curve has index
\mu(c_{+})-\mu(c_{-})-(\mu_{\mathrm{CZ}}(q^{\prime\prime})+n-2)
, which is negative by (
A.1
). In the second case, the upper-right curve has index
\mu_{Y}(q)-\mu(c_{-})-1-(\mu_{\mathrm{RS}}(c^{\prime})+\frac{n-2}{2})
, which is negative again by (
A.1
). Thus neither case can occur, and this finishes the proof.
∎

We may assume that
f_{\mathcal{L}}
has a unique minimum point
c
. Then, for a sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
, the canonical orientation
o_{c}\in\mathrm{FC}_{n}^{(b_{1},b_{2})}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{\varepsilon})
, where
b_{1}<0<b_{2}
, associated to
c
is a cycle. We write
1_{\mathrm{RFH}}\in\mathrm{RFH}_{n}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
for the limit of
[o_{c}]
under continuation homomorphisms. In other words, the unit of
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathcal{L})
maps to
1_{\mathrm{RFH}}
in the limit. It serves as the unit for the triangle product on
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
when this product structure is well-defined.

Proposition A.3
.

We assume the index conditions in (
A.1
). If
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)=0
, then, for any
\mathrm{Pin}^{\pm}
-structure
\tilde{\sigma}
on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{L}
,
1_{\mathrm{RFH}}\in\mathrm{RFH}_{n}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}})
is zero.

Proof.

Let
a>0
be sufficiently large. Let
\mathfrak{c}:\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{\varepsilon})\to\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{a+\varepsilon})
be a continuation homomorphism. Here and below,
\mathrm{FC}_{*}
denotes
\mathrm{FC}_{*}^{(-\infty,\infty)}
. We show that
\mathfrak{c}(o_{c})
is a boundary.

We may assume that
f_{Y}
has a unique minimum point
p\in Y
. Then the canonical orientation
o_{p}\in\mathrm{FC}_{n+1}(H_{\varepsilon})
associated to
p
is a cycle for a sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
. Note that
o_{p}
represents the unit when the pair of pants product is well-defined. Let
\mathfrak{c}_{Y}:\mathrm{FC}_{*}(H_{\varepsilon})\to\mathrm{FC}_{*}(H_{a})
be a continuation homomorphism. Since
\mathrm{RFH}_{*}(Y)=0
,
\mathfrak{c}_{Y}(o_{p})
is a boundary in
\mathrm{FC}_{*}(H_{a})
. Thus, there exists
\xi\in\mathrm{FC}_{n+2}(H_{a})
such that
\partial_{Y}(\xi)=\mathfrak{c}_{Y}(o_{p})
, where
\partial_{Y}
is the boundary map in
\mathrm{FC}_{*}(H_{a})
. We consider the following diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FC}_{*}(H_{a})\otimes\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{a+\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FC}_{*}(H_{a})\otimes\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{a+\varepsilon})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{Y}\otimes\mathrm{id}\pm\mathrm{id}\otimes\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{2}}

Here,
\mathfrak{m}_{2}
is defined via the moduli spaces in (
A.2
). It induces a module structure on homology when
\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}}
has an exact Lagrangian filling. By Proposition
A.2
, this diagram commutes when restricted to
\xi\otimes o_{c}\in\mathrm{FC}_{n+2}(H_{a})\otimes\mathrm{FC}_{n}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{\varepsilon})
:

\begin{split}\partial\circ\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\xi\otimes o_{c})=\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\partial_{Y}(\xi)\otimes o_{c})=\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\mathfrak{c}_{Y}(o_{p})\otimes o_{c}).\end{split}

(A.3)

Similarly,
\mathfrak{m}_{1}
denotes the corresponding chain-level map defined using the Hamiltonians
(H_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon},H_{2\varepsilon})
.
Then, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FC}_{*}(H_{\varepsilon})\otimes\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{2\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FC}_{*}(H_{a})\otimes\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{a+\varepsilon})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{c}_{Y}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{m}_{2}}

commutes up to homotopy when restricted to
o_{p}\otimes o_{c}
by an argument analogous to that of Proposition
A.2
. Let
\mathcal{H}
denote the corresponding chain homotopy. Since both
o_{p}
and
o_{c}
are cycles, we have

\mathfrak{c}\circ\mathfrak{m}_{1}(o_{p}\otimes o_{c})=\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\mathfrak{c}_{Y}(o_{p})\otimes o_{c})+\partial\circ\mathcal{H}(o_{p}\otimes o_{c}).

(A.4)

By energy considerations,
\mathcal{M}(p,c,c^{\prime};H_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon},H_{2\varepsilon})=\emptyset
for
c^{\prime}\neq c
, and
\mathcal{M}(p,c,c;H_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon},H_{2\varepsilon})
consists only of the constant curve along
c
, and thus
\mathfrak{m}_{1}(o_{p}\otimes o_{c})=o_{c}
. This together with (
A.3
) and (
A.4
) yields

\mathfrak{c}(o_{c})=\mathfrak{c}\circ\mathfrak{m}_{1}(o_{p}\otimes o_{c})=\partial(\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\xi\otimes o_{c})+\mathcal{H}(o_{p}\otimes o_{c})).

Hence,
\mathfrak{c}(o_{c})
is a boundary in
\mathrm{FC}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{a+\varepsilon})
and also in
\mathrm{FC}_{*}^{(b_{1},b_{2})}(\mathcal{L}^{\tilde{\sigma}};H_{a+\varepsilon})
for
b_{1}<0\ll b_{2}
. This proves
1_{\mathrm{RFH}}=0
as desired.
∎
